## A Toy Princess / 玩具公主

===Original===
More than a thousand years ago, in a country quite on the other side of the world, it fell out that the people all grew so very polite that they hardly ever spoke to each other. And they never said more than was quite necessary, as “Just so,” “Yes indeed,” “Thank you,” and “If you please.” And it was thought to be the rudest thing in the world for anyone to say they liked or disliked, or loved or hated, or were happy or miserable. No one ever laughed aloud, and if anyone had been seen to cry they would at once have been avoided by their friends.

===Chinese===
一千多年前，在世界的另一头有一个国家，那里的人们变得彬彬有礼过了头，结果彼此之间几乎绝口不说话。他们绝不多说一句必要之外的话，无非是“正是如此”“的确如此”“谢谢”和“劳驾”这么几句。谁要是说出自己喜欢什么、讨厌什么，爱什么、恨什么，是快活还是难过，都被认为是天下最失礼的事。谁也不放声大笑；要是叫人瞧见谁哭了，朋友们立刻就会躲开他。

===Original===
The King of this country married a Princess from a neighbouring land, who was very good and beautiful, but the people in her own home were as unlike her husband’s people as it was possible to be. They laughed, and talked, and were noisy and merry when they were happy, and cried and lamented if they were sad. In fact, whatever they felt they showed at once, and the Princess was just like them.

===Chinese===
这个国家的国王娶了一位邻国公主。公主又善良又美丽，只是她娘家的人同她丈夫的臣民，恰恰是天下最不像的两路人：他们高兴时又说又笑、热热闹闹、欢欢喜喜，悲伤时就放声痛哭、哀哀悲叹。总之，心里有什么感触，脸上就立刻现出什么，公主也正是这脾气。

===Original===
So when she came to her new home, she could not at all understand her subjects, or make out why there was no shouting and cheering to welcome her, and why everyone was so distant and formal. After a time, when she found they never changed, but were always the same, just as stiff and quiet, she wept, and began to pine for her own old home.

===Chinese===
于是到了新家，她全然不能理解她的臣民，也弄不明白为什么没有欢呼喝彩来迎接她，为什么人人都这样疏远、这样拘谨。过了一阵子，她见他们从不变通，永远是老样子，永远那样呆板沉默，便哭了，思念起自己的故土来。

===Original===
Every day she grew thinner and paler. The courtiers were much too polite to notice how ill their young Queen looked; but she knew it herself, and believed she was going to die.

===Chinese===
她一天比一天消瘦，一天比一天苍白。廷臣们礼数太重，谁也不肯点破年轻王后病成了什么样子；可她自己心里明白，觉得自己是活不成了。

===Original===
Now she had a fairy godmother, named Taboret, whom she loved very dearly, and who was always kind to her. When she knew her end was drawing near she sent for her godmother, and when she came had a long talk with her quite alone.

===Chinese===
她有一位仙女教母，名叫塔博蕾（Taboret），她非常敬爱教母，教母也一向待她慈爱。她知道自己大限将至，便派人去请教母来；教母到了以后，两人单独密谈了许久。

===Original===
No one knew what was said, and soon afterwards a little Princess was born, and the Queen died. Of course all the courtiers were sorry for the poor Queen’s death, but it would have been thought rude to say so. So, although there was a grand funeral, and the court put on mourning, everything else went on much as it had done before.

===Chinese===
没有人知道她们说了些什么。不久，一位小公主降生了，王后便去世了。廷臣们自然都哀怜可怜的王后之死，但说出来便会被认为失礼。所以，尽管举行了盛大的葬礼，宫廷也挂了孝，其余一切照旧，同从前没什么两样。

===Original===
The little baby was christened Ursula, and given to some court ladies to be taken charge of. Poor little Princess! She cried hard enough, and nothing could stop her.

===Chinese===
小婴儿行了洗礼，取名厄休拉（Ursula），交给几位宫廷女官照料。可怜的小公主！她哭得可凶了，怎么也止不住。

===Original===
All her ladies were frightened, and said that they had not heard such a dreadful noise for a long time. But, till she was about two years old, nothing could stop her crying when she was cold or hungry, or crowing when she was pleased.

===Chinese===
女官们都吓坏了，说这么可怕的声音，她们许久没有听见过。可是直到两岁上下，她冷了饿了照旧哭个不停，高兴了照旧咯咯直笑，谁也拿她没法子。

===Original===
After that she began to understand a little what was meant when her nurses told her, in cold, polite tones, that she was being naughty, and she grew much quieter.

===Chinese===
打那以后，乳母们用冷冰冰、客客气气的声调说她不听话，她渐渐能听懂一点了，也就安静了许多。

===Original===
She was a pretty little girl, with a round baby face and big merry blue eyes; but as she grew older, her eyes grew less and less merry and bright, and her fat little face grew thin and pale. She was not allowed to play with any other children, lest she might learn bad manners; and she was not taught any games or given any toys. So she passed most of her time, when she was not at her lessons, looking out of the window at the birds flying against the clear blue sky; and sometimes she would give a sad little sigh when her ladies were not listening.

===Chinese===
她是个漂亮的小姑娘，圆圆的娃娃脸，一双快活的蓝色大眼睛。可她越长越大，那双眼睛的快活和神采却越来越少，胖乎乎的小脸也渐渐消瘦、苍白。不许她同别的孩子一起玩，怕她学坏规矩；也没人教她游戏，没人给她玩具。于是，不上课的时候，她多半是望着窗外，看鸟儿在晴朗的碧空里飞翔；有时趁女官们不留神，她会小小地、伤心地叹一口气。

===Original===
One day the old fairy Taboret made herself invisible, and flew over to the King’s palace to see how things were going on there. She went straight up to the nursery, where she found poor little Ursula sitting by the window, with her head leaning on her hand.

===Chinese===
一天，老仙女塔博蕾隐去了身形，飞到国王的宫殿，看看那边情形如何。她径直上了育婴室，只见可怜的小厄休拉坐在窗边，一手托着腮。

===Original===
It was a very grand room, but there were no toys or dolls about, and when the fairy saw this, she frowned to herself and shook her head.

===Chinese===
那是一间极华丽的屋子，可四下里没有一件玩具、一个布娃娃。仙女见了，暗暗皱眉，摇了摇头。

===Original===
“Your Royal Highness’s dinner is now ready,” said the head nurse to Ursula.

===Chinese===
“殿下的晚饭已经备好了。”保姆长对厄休拉说。

===Original===
“I don’t want any dinner,” said Ursula, without turning her head.

===Chinese===
“我不想吃饭。”厄休拉说，头也不回。

===Original===
“I think I have told your Royal Highness before that it is not polite to say you don’t want anything, or that you don’t like it,” said the nurse. “We are waiting for your Royal Highness.”

===Chinese===
“殿下，我记得早禀告过了，说自己不想吃什么、不喜欢什么，都是不礼貌的。”保姆说，“我们都候着殿下呢。”

===Original===
So the Princess got up and went to the dinner-table, and Taboret watched them all the time. When she saw how pale little Ursula was, and how little she ate, and that there was no talking or laughing allowed, she sighed and frowned even more than before, and then she flew back to her fairy home, where she sat for some hours in deep thought.

===Chinese===
于是公主起身走向餐桌，塔博蕾在一旁看着这一切。她见小厄休拉脸色那样苍白，吃得那样少，席间又不许说笑，不由得叹了口气，眉头皱得更紧了。随后她飞回仙府，坐在那里沉思了好几个钟头。

===Original===
At last she rose, and went out to pay a visit to the largest shop in Fairyland.

===Chinese===
最后她站起身，动身去拜访仙境（Fairyland）最大的一家铺子。

===Original===
It was a queer sort of shop. It was neither a grocer’s, nor a draper’s, nor a hatter’s. Yet it contained sugar, and dresses, and hats. But the sugar was magic sugar, which transformed any liquid into which it was put; the dresses each had some special charm, and the hats were wishing-caps. It was, in fact, a shop where every sort of spell or charm was sold.

===Chinese===
那是一家古怪的铺子。它既不是杂货铺，也不是绸缎庄，更不是帽子店，可铺子里又有糖，有衣裳，有帽子。只是那糖是魔法糖，放进什么液体里，什么液体就要变形；那些衣裳每一件都带着特别的魔力，帽子则全是许愿帽。其实，这是一家出售各式咒语法术的铺子。

===Original===
Into this shop Taboret flew; and as she was well known there as a good customer, the master of the shop came forward to meet her at once, and bowing, begged to know what he could get for her.

===Chinese===
塔博蕾飞进这家铺子。她是这儿有名的老主顾，店主一见她便迎上前来，躬着身问她要点什么货。

===Original===
“I want,” said Taboret, “a Princess.”

===Chinese===
“我要，”塔博蕾说，“一位公主。”

===Original===
“A Princess!” said the shopman, who was in reality an old wizard. “What size do you want it? I have one or two in stock.”

===Chinese===
“一位公主！”店伙说——他其实就是个老巫师。“您要多大尺寸的？我手头现成一两位都有。”

===Original===
“It must look now about six years old. But it must grow.”

===Chinese===
“现在看上去要有六岁上下。可她还必须会长。”

===Original===
“I can make you one,” said the wizard, “but it’ll come rather expensive.”

===Chinese===
“我可以给您做一个，”巫师说，“不过价钱可不便宜。”

===Original===
“I don’t mind that,” said Taboret. “See! I want it to look exactly like this,” and so saying she took a portrait of Ursula out of her bosom and gave it to the old man, who examined it carefully.

===Chinese===
“那我不在乎，”塔博蕾说，“瞧！我要她同这个一模一样。”说着，她从怀里取出一张厄休拉的画像，递给老人，老人细细端详了一番。

===Original===
“I’ll get it for you,” he said. “When will you want it?”

===Chinese===
“我给您办妥。”他说，“您几时要？”

===Original===
“As soon as possible,” said Taboret. “By tomorrow evening if possible. How much will it cost?”

===Chinese===
“越快越好，”塔博蕾说，“最好明晚就要。要多少钱？”

===Original===
“It’ll come to a good deal,” said the wizard, thoughtfully. “I have such difficulty in getting these things properly made in these days. What sort of a voice is it to have?”

===Chinese===
“那可得花一大笔，”巫师若有所思地说，“如今这年月，要把这种东西做得像样，实在不容易。她要一副什么样的嗓子？”

===Original===
“It need not be at all talkative,” said Taboret, “so that won’t add much to the price. It need only say, ‘If you please,’ ‘No, thank you,’ ‘Certainly,’ and ‘Just so.’ ”

===Chinese===
“用不着多话，”塔博蕾说，“这倒加不了几个钱。她只要会说‘劳驾’‘不，谢谢’‘当然’和‘正是如此’就够了。”

===Original===
“Well, under those circumstances,” said the wizard, “I will do it for four cat’s footfalls, two fish’s screams, and two swan’s songs.”

===Chinese===
“好吧，既然这样，”巫师说，“四声猫的脚步、两声鱼的尖叫，再加两支天鹅的歌，我就给您做。”

===Original===
“It is too much,” cried Taboret. “I’ll give you the footfalls and the screams, but to ask for swans’ songs!”

===Chinese===
“太贵了！”塔博蕾叫道，“脚步和尖叫我都依你，居然还要天鹅的歌！”

===Original===
She did not really think it dear, but she always made a point of trying to beat tradesmen down.

===Chinese===
她其实并不觉得贵，只是她向来有个规矩，总要跟做买卖的讲讲价。

===Original===
“I can’t do it for less,” said the wizard, “and if you think it too much, you’d better try another shop.”

===Chinese===
“少一个子儿也不行，”巫师说，“您嫌贵，就请上别家铺子问去。”

===Original===
“As I am really in a hurry for it, and cannot spend time in searching about, I suppose I must have it,” said Taboret; “but I consider the price very high. When will it be ready?”

===Chinese===
“我实在是急等着用，没工夫挨家去寻，”塔博蕾说，“看来只好依你了；可我认为这价钱实在太高。几时能做好？”

===Original===
“By tomorrow evening.”

===Chinese===
“明晚。”

===Original===
“Very well, then, be sure it is ready for me by the time I call for it, and whatever you do, don’t make it at all noisy or rough in its ways;” and Taboret swept out of the shop and returned to her home.

===Chinese===
“那么好，我来取的时候，千万要备妥了。还有，无论如何，别把她做得爱吵闹、毛手毛脚。”说罢，塔博蕾一甩身子出了铺子，回家去了。

===Original===
Next evening she returned and asked if her job was done.

===Chinese===
第二天晚上她又来了，问她的活计做好了没有。

===Original===
“I will fetch it, and I am sure you will like it,” said the wizard, leaving the shop as he spoke. Presently he came back, leading by the hand a pretty little girl of about six years old﻿—a little girl so like the Princess Ursula that no one could have told them apart.

===Chinese===
“我去把她领来，保管您中意。”巫师说着便出了铺子。不多时他回来了，手里牵着一个六岁模样的漂亮小姑娘——那小姑娘同厄休拉公主像得叫人分辨不出彼此。

===Original===
“Well,” said Taboret, “it looks well enough. But are you sure that it’s a good piece of workmanship, and won’t give way anywhere?”

===Chinese===
“嗯，”塔博蕾说，“模样倒还周正。可你保准这活计做得结实，哪儿都不会散架吗？”

===Original===
“It’s as good a piece of work as ever was done,” said the wizard, proudly, striking the child on the back as he spoke, “Look at it! Examine it all over, and see if you find a flaw anywhere. There’s not one fairy in twenty who could tell it from the real thing, and no mortal could.”

===Chinese===
“这活计做得天下少有，”巫师骄傲地说，一边在那孩子背上拍了一下，“您瞧瞧！上上下下仔细验一验，看能不能挑出一处毛病。二十个仙女里也挑不出一个能认出她是假的，凡人就更是想都别想了。”

===Original===
“It seems to be fairly made,” said Taboret, approvingly, as she turned the little girl round. “Now I’ll pay you, and then will be off;” with which she raised her wand in the air and waved it three times, and there arose a series of strange sounds.

===Chinese===
“做工还算过得去。”塔博蕾说着，赞许地把小姑娘转过身来看了看。“现在付钱，付完就走。”说着她把魔杖向空中一举，挥了三下，顿时响起一串奇怪的声音。

===Original===
The first was a low tramping, the second shrill and piercing screams, the third voices of wonderful beauty, singing a very sorrowful song.

===Chinese===
头一声是低低的踏步声，第二声是尖利刺耳的尖叫，第三声是美妙无比的歌喉，唱着一支十分哀伤的歌。

===Original===
The wizard caught all the sounds and pocketed them at once, and Taboret, without ceremony, picked up the child, took her head downwards under her arm, and flew away.

===Chinese===
巫师把那些声音一一接住，立刻装进了口袋。塔博蕾也不客气，拎起那孩子，头朝下往腋下一夹，飞走了。

===Original===
At court that night the little Princess had been naughty, and had refused to go to bed. It was a long time before her ladies could get her into her crib, and when she was there, she did not really go to sleep, only lay still and pretended, till everyone went away; then she got up and stole noiselessly to the window, and sat down on the window-seat all curled up in a little bunch, while she looked out wistfully at the moon. She was such a pretty soft little thing, with all her warm bright hair falling over her shoulders, that it would have been hard for most people to be angry with her. She leaned her chin on her tiny white hands, and as she gazed out, the tears rose to her great blue eyes; but remembering that her ladies would call this naughty, she wiped them hastily away with her nightgown sleeve.

===Chinese===
这天夜里，宫里的小公主不听话，闹着不肯睡觉。女官们费了好大的劲才把她哄进小床，可她上了床也没有真睡，只静静躺着装睡，等人都走了，她就爬起来，蹑手蹑脚地溜到窗前，蜷成小小一团坐在窗座上，怅然望着月亮。她是那样一个娇软可爱的小东西，一头暖色明亮的头发披散在肩上，多数人见了都很难对她生气。她把下巴搁在白白的小手上，望着望着，泪珠涌上了蓝色的大眼睛；可她想起女官们会说这是淘气，便赶紧用睡衣的袖子把泪擦掉了。

===Original===
“Ah moon, pretty bright moon!” she said to herself, “I wonder if they let you cry when you want to. I think I’d like to go up there and live with you; I’m sure it would be nicer than being here.”

===Chinese===
“啊，月亮，又亮又美的月亮！”她自言自语道，“不知道你要哭的时候，他们让不让你哭。我想，我情愿上你那儿去，同你一起住；那一定比待在这儿好。”

===Original===
“Would you like to go away with me?” said a voice close beside her; and looking up she saw a funny old woman in a red cloak, standing near to her. She was not frightened, for the old woman had a kind smile and bright black eyes, though her nose was hooked and her chin long.

===Chinese===
“愿意跟我走吗？”她身旁一个声音说道。她抬起头，看见一个滑稽的老婆婆站在近旁，披着红斗篷。她并不害怕，因为老婆婆虽然长着鹰钩鼻子、长下巴，笑容却很慈祥，一双眼又黑又亮。

===Original===
“Where would you take me?” said the little Princess, sucking her thumb, and staring with all her might.

===Chinese===
“你要带我去哪儿？”小公主嘬着大拇指，使足了劲盯着她问。

===Original===
“I’d take you to the seashore, where you’d be able to play about on the sands, and where you’d have-some little boys and girls to play with, and no one to tell you not to make a noise.”

===Chinese===
“我带你到海边去，你可以在沙滩上跑来跑去地玩儿，还有几个小男孩小女孩陪你玩儿，谁也不会叫你别吵闹。”

===Original===
“I’ll go,” cried Ursula, springing up at once.

===Chinese===
“我愿意去！”厄休拉喊道，一下子跳了起来。

===Original===
“Come along,” said the old woman, taking her tenderly in her arms and folding her in her warm red cloak. Then they rose up in the air, and flew out of the window, right away over the tops of the houses.

===Chinese===
“那就走吧。”老婆婆说着，温柔地把她抱进怀里，用暖和的红斗篷把她裹住。随后两人腾空而起，飞出窗外，越过一重重屋顶，一直飞远了。

===Original===
The night air was sharp, and Ursula soon fell asleep; but still they kept flying on, on, over hill and dale, for miles and miles, away from the palace, towards the sea.

===Chinese===
夜里的空气冷冽，厄休拉很快就睡着了；可她们还在不停地飞呀飞，翻山越谷，飞了一程又一程，离宫殿越来越远，向着大海飞去。

===Original===
Far away from the court and the palace, in a tiny fishing village, on the sea, was a little hut where a fisherman named Mark lived with his wife and three children. He was a poor man, and lived on the fish he caught in his little boat. The children, Oliver, Philip, and little Bell, were rosy-cheeked and bright-eyed. They played all day long on the shore, and shouted till they were hoarse. To this village the fairy bore the still sleeping Ursula, and gently placed her on the doorstep of Mark’s cottage; then she kissed her cheeks, and with one gust blew the door open, and disappeared before anyone could come to see who it was.

===Chinese===
远离宫廷的地方，海边有一座小小的渔村，村里一间小屋里住着渔夫马克（Mark）和他的妻子、三个孩子。他家境贫寒，全靠小船捕来的鱼过活。三个孩子奥利弗（Oliver）、菲利普（Philip）和小贝尔（Bell），个个脸颊红润、眼睛明亮，整天在海滩上玩耍，喊叫得嗓子都哑了。仙女抱着熟睡未醒的厄休拉来到这村子，轻轻把她放在马克家的门槛上，亲了亲她的脸颊，又一口气把门吹开，趁人还没出来看清是谁，就隐去了身影。

===Original===
The fisherman and his wife were sitting quietly within. She was making the children clothes, and he was mending his net, when without any noise the door opened and the cold night air blew in.

===Chinese===
渔夫和妻子正安安静静坐在屋里。她在给孩子们缝衣裳，他在补鱼网，忽听得门悄没声儿地开了，夜里的寒气灌了进来。

===Original===
“Wife,” said the fisherman, “just see who’s at the door.”

===Chinese===
“孩子他妈，”渔夫说，“去看看门口是谁。”

===Original===
The wife got up and went to the door, and there lay Ursula, still sleeping soundly, in her little white nightdress.

===Chinese===
妻子起身走到门口，只见厄休拉躺在那儿，穿着小小的白睡裙，还睡得正香。

===Original===
The woman gave a little scream at sight of the child, and called to her husband.

===Chinese===
那女人一见这孩子，轻轻尖叫了一声，忙喊她丈夫。

===Original===
“Husband, see, here’s a little girl!” and so saying she lifted her in her arms, and carried her into the cottage. When she was brought into the warmth and light, Ursula awoke, and sitting up, stared about her in fright. She did not cry, as another child might have done, but she trembled very much, and was almost too frightened to speak.

===Chinese===
“当家的，快瞧，有个小姑娘！”说着她把她抱起来，抱进屋里。一到暖和亮堂的屋里，厄休拉就醒了，坐起身，惊慌地四下张望。她没有像别的孩子那样哭，只是抖得厉害，吓得几乎说不出话来。

===Original===
Oddly enough, she had forgotten all about her strange flight through the air, and could remember nothing to tell the fisherman and his wife, but that she was the Princess Ursula; and, on hearing this, the good man and woman thought the poor little girl must be a trifle mad. However, when they examined her little nightdress, made of white fine linen and embroidery, with a crown worked in one corner, they agreed that she must belong to very grand people. They said it would be cruel to send the poor little thing away on such a cold night, and they must of course keep her till she was claimed. So the woman gave her some warm bread-and-milk, and put her to bed with their own little girl.

===Chinese===
奇怪的是，她把那趟空中奇异的飞行忘了个干净，除了自己是厄休拉公主之外，什么也说不出来给渔夫夫妇听。两口子一听，心想这可怜的小姑娘只怕脑筋有点毛病。可他们细看她那件小睡裙——上好的白亚麻布，绣着花，一角还绣着一顶王冠——便认定她必是大富大贵人家的孩子。他们说，这么冷的天把可怜的小东西赶出门去，未免太狠心了，自然得留下她，等她家里人来认领。于是女人给她吃了些热乎的牛奶泡面包，安排她同自家的小女儿一起睡了。

===Original===
In the morning, when the court ladies came to wake Princess Ursula, they found her sleeping as usual in her little bed, and little did they think it was not she, but a toy Princess placed there in her stead. Indeed the ladies were much pleased; for when they said, “It is time for your Royal Highness to arise,” she only answered, “Certainly,” and let herself be dressed without another word. And as the time passed, and she was never naughty, and scarcely ever spoke, all said she was vastly improved, and she grew to be a great favourite.

===Chinese===
早晨，宫廷女官们来唤醒厄休拉公主，见她同往常一样睡在小床上，万万想不到那已不是她，而是一个顶替她的玩具公主。女官们实在欢喜：她们说“殿下该起身了”，她只答一声“当然”，便一言不发任人穿戴。日子久了，她从不出岔子，几乎从不说话，人人夸她大有长进，她成了众人的心头宠。

===Original===
The ladies all said that the young Princess bid fair to have the most elegant manners in the country, and the King smiled and noticed her with pleasure.

===Chinese===
女官们都说，小公主将来必是全国举止最优雅的人，国王也面带笑容，欢喜地留意她。

===Original===
In the meantime, in the fisherman’s cottage far away, the real Ursula grew tall and straight as an alder, and merry and lighthearted as a bird.

===Chinese===
这时候，在远方渔夫的小屋里，真正的厄休拉出落得像赤杨一样挺拔，像小鸟一样快活无忧。

===Original===
No one came to claim her, so the good fisherman and his wife kept her and brought her up among their own little ones. She played with them on the beach, and learned her lessons with them at school, and her old life had become like a dream she barely remembered.

===Chinese===
没有人来认领她，好心的渔夫夫妇便留下她，同自己的孩子们一起抚养。她同他们在海滩上玩耍，同他们一起上学念书，从前的生活渐渐像一场梦，她几乎记不得了。

===Original===
But sometimes the mother would take out the little embroidered nightgown and show it to her, and wonder whence she came, and to whom she belonged.

===Chinese===
只是有时候，渔夫的妻子会取出那件绣花小睡裙给她看，心里疑惑她究竟从哪里来，是谁家的孩子。

===Original===
“I don’t care who I belong to,” said Ursula; “they won’t come and take me from you, and that’s all I care about.” So she grew tall and fair, and as she grew, the toy Princess, in her place at the court, grew too, and always was just like her, only that whereas Ursula’s face was sunburnt and her cheeks red, the face of the toy Princess was pale, with only a very slight tint in her cheeks.

===Chinese===
“我不管我是谁家的，”厄休拉说，“反正他们不会来把我从你们身边带走，我只管这个。”就这样，她越长越高，越长越漂亮。她长，宫里顶替她的那个玩具公主也跟着长，模样总是同她一模一样，只是厄休拉的脸晒得黑红的，两颊红扑扑，玩具公主的脸却白净净的，两颊只隐隐透出一点血色。

===Original===
Years passed, and Ursula at the cottage was a tall young woman, and Ursula at the court was thought to be the most beautiful there, and everyone admired her manners, though she never said anything but “If you please,” “No, thank you,” “Certainly,” and “Just so.”

===Chinese===
一年年过去，小屋里的厄休拉长成了亭亭玉立的少女；宫里的厄休拉也被公认为宫中第一美人，人人称道她的举止，尽管她除了“劳驾”“不，谢谢”“当然”和“正是如此”，从来不曾说别的。

===Original===
The King was now an old man, and the fisherman Mark and his wife were grey-headed. Most of their fishing was now done by their eldest son, Oliver, who was their great pride. Ursula waited on them, and cleaned the house, and did the needlework, and was so useful that they could not have done without her. The fairy Taboret had come to the cottage from time to time, unseen by anyone, to see Ursula, and always finding her healthy and merry, was pleased to think of how she had saved her from a dreadful life. But one evening when she paid them a visit, not having been there for some time, she saw something which made her pause and consider. Oliver and Ursula were standing together watching the waves, and Taboret stopped to hear what they said﻿—

===Chinese===
国王已经老了，渔夫马克和妻子也白了头。打鱼的事多半交给了大儿子奥利弗，他是老两口的骄傲。厄休拉伺候二老，收拾屋子，做针线活，处处得力，老两口离了她简直不行。仙女塔博蕾时不时隐身到小屋来看厄休拉，每回见她健康快活，想到自己把她从一种可怕的生活里救了出来，心里很是欣慰。可是有天傍晚，她隔了好些日子再来，却看见了叫她驻足沉思的一幕：奥利弗和厄休拉并肩站着看海浪，塔博蕾停下来听他们说些什么——

===Original===
“When we are married,” said Oliver, softly, “we will live in that little cottage yonder, so that we can come and see them every day. But that will not be till little Bell is old enough to take your place, for how would my mother do without you?”

===Chinese===
“等我们成了亲，”奥利弗柔声说，“就住到那边那座小屋里去，好天天回来看望他们。不过那得等小贝尔长大了能接替你才行，要不然，我娘离了你可怎么过呢？”

===Original===
“And we had better not tell them,” said Ursula, “that we mean to marry, or else the thought that they are preventing us will make them unhappy.”

===Chinese===
“咱们还是先别告诉他们我们要成亲的事，”厄休拉说，“不然他们想着是自己拦着咱们，心里会难过的。”

===Original===
When Taboret heard this she became grave, and pondered for a long time. At last she flew back to the court to see how things were going on there. She found the King in the middle of a state council. On seeing this, she at once made herself visible, when the King begged her to be seated near him, as he was always glad of her help and advice.

===Chinese===
塔博蕾听了，神色郑重起来，思量了许久。最后她飞回宫去，看看那边情形如何。她见国王正开枢密会议，便立刻现了身形。国王忙请她坐到自己身边，因为她的鼎力相助和金玉良言，是他一向求之不得的。

===Original===
“You find us,” said his Majesty, “just about to resign our sceptre into younger and more vigorous hands; in fact, we think we are growing too old to reign, and mean to abdicate in favour of our dear daughter, who will reign in our stead.”

===Chinese===
“您来得正巧，”陛下说，“我们正要把权杖交到更年轻、更有活力的手里。不瞒您说，我们自觉年老，不堪再执掌王位，打算逊位给我们的爱女，让她代我们统治。”

===Original===
“Before you do any such thing,” said Taboret, “just let me have a little private conversation with you;” and she led the King into a corner, much to his surprise and alarm.

===Chinese===
“您且慢这么办，”塔博蕾说，“先让我同您私下说几句话。”说着便把国王领到一个角落里，弄得他又惊又怕。

===Original===
In about half an hour he returned to the council, looking very white, and with a dreadful expression on his face, whilst he held a handkerchief to his eyes.

===Chinese===
约莫半个钟头后，他回到议席上，脸色惨白，神情可怕，一方手帕掩着眼睛。

===Original===
“My lords,” he faltered, “pray pardon our apparently extraordinary behaviour. We have just received a dreadful blow; we hear on authority, which we cannot doubt, that our dear, dear daughter”﻿—here sobs choked his voice, and he was almost unable to proceed﻿—“is﻿—is﻿—in fact, not our daughter at all, and only a *sham*.” Here the King sank back in his chair, overpowered with grief, and the fairy Taboret, stepping to the front, told the courtiers the whole story; how she had stolen the real Princess, because she feared they were spoiling her, and how she had placed a toy Princess in her place. The courtiers looked from one to another in surprise, but it was evident they did not believe her.

===Chinese===
“列位爱卿，”他哆哆嗦嗦地说，“恕我们这般失态。我们刚遭了一个可怕的打击：据确凿无疑的消息，我们亲爱的、亲爱的女儿”——说到这里，呜咽噎住了他的喉咙，他几乎说不下去——“她——她——其实根本不是我们的女儿，只是个*冒牌货*。”国王说完，悲伤得瘫倒在椅子里。仙女塔博蕾走上前来，把事情原原本本告诉了廷臣们：她怎样偷走了真公主，因为怕他们把她娇惯坏了；又怎样放了一个玩具公主顶替她。廷臣们面面相觑，满脸惊愕，可他们分明不信她的话。

===Original===
“The Princess is a truly charming young lady,” said the Prime Minister.

===Chinese===
“公主是位真正惹人喜爱的少女。”首相说。

===Original===
“Has your Majesty any reason to complain of her Royal Highness’s conduct?” asked the old Chancellor.

===Chinese===
“陛下可曾有什么缘由，要怪罪殿下的行止吗？”老宰相问。

===Original===
“None whatever,” sobbed the King; “she was ever an excellent daughter.”

===Chinese===
“从来没有，”国王呜咽道，“她从来是个孝顺的好女儿。”

===Original===
“Then I don’t see,” said the Chancellor, “what reason your Majesty can have for paying any attention to what this﻿—this person says.”

===Chinese===
“那么恕老臣直言，”宰相说，“陛下还有什么理由，要把这——这位的话当回事呢？”

===Original===
“If you don’t believe me, you old idiots,” cried Taboret, “call the Princess here, and I’ll soon prove my words.”

===Chinese===
“你们这些老糊涂，要是不信我，”塔博蕾喊道，“就把公主叫来，我立刻证明给你们看。”

===Original===
“By all means,” cried they.

===Chinese===
“那再好不过。”他们齐声喊道。

===Original===
So the King commanded that her Royal Highness should be summoned.

===Chinese===
于是国王下旨，宣公主上殿。

===Original===
In a few minutes she came, attended by her ladies. She said nothing, but then she never did speak till she was spoken to. So she entered, and stood in the middle of the room silently.

===Chinese===
不多时她来了，身后跟着女官们。她一言不发——本来她也从不先开口，总要等人先对她说话。她走进来，默默站在大殿中央。

===Original===
“We have desired that your presence be requested,” the King was beginning, but Taboret without any ceremony advanced towards her, and struck her lightly on the head with her wand. In a moment the head rolled on the floor, leaving the body standing motionless as before, and showing that it was but an empty shell. “Just so,” said the head, as it rolled towards the King, and he and the courtiers nearly swooned with fear.

===Chinese===
“我们方才命人请殿下前来——”国王刚开口，塔博蕾却毫不客气地抢上前去，用魔杖在她头上轻轻一敲。那颗头颅登时滚落在地，身子却仍旧一动不动站在原处，分明只是一具空壳。“正是如此。”那头颅一边朝国王滚过去一边说。国王和廷臣们吓得差点晕过去。

===Original===
When they were a little recovered, the King spoke again. “The fairy tells me,” he said, “that there is somewhere a real Princess whom she wishes us to adopt as our daughter. And in the meantime let her Royal Highness be carefully placed in a cupboard, and a general mourning be proclaimed for this dire event.”

===Chinese===
等他们稍稍定下神来，国王又开口了。“仙女告诉我，”他说，“说世上还有一位真公主，她希望我们收为女儿。眼下，且请把殿下小心安放到橱柜里去，并为这一惨事下令举国服丧。”

===Original===
So saying he glanced tenderly at the body and head, and turned weeping away.

===Chinese===
说着，他深情地望了一眼那身躯和头颅，掩面哭着转过身去。

===Original===
So it was settled that Taboret was to fetch Princess Ursula, and the King and council were to be assembled to meet her.

===Chinese===
事情就这样定了：由塔博蕾去接厄休拉公主回来，国王和枢密大臣齐集一堂迎候她。

===Original===
That evening the fairy flew to Mark’s cottage, and told them the whole truth about Ursula, and that they must part from her.

===Chinese===
当天晚上，仙女飞到马克家，把厄休拉的真情实况说了，又说他们必须同她分别了。

===Original===
Loud were their lamentations, and great their grief, when they heard she must leave them. Poor Ursula herself sobbed bitterly.

===Chinese===
听说她必须离开，一家人哭声震天，悲恸万分。可怜的厄休拉自己也哭得死去活来。

===Original===
“Never mind,” she cried after a time, “if I am really a great Princess, I will have you all to live with me. I am sure the King, my father, will wish it, when he hears how good you have all been to me.”

===Chinese===
“别难过，”过了一会儿她喊道，“要是我真是位尊贵的公主，我就接你们全家都来同我一起住。我父王听说了你们大家待我的好处，一定乐意的。”

===Original===
On the appointed day, Taboret came for Ursula in a grand coach and four, and drove her away to the court. It was a long, long drive; and she stopped on the way and had the Princess dressed in a splendid white silk dress trimmed with gold, and put pearls round her neck and in her hair, that she might appear properly at court.

===Chinese===
到了约定的日子，塔博蕾乘一辆华丽的四驾马车来接厄休拉，载着她往王宫去。车走了很久很久；半路上她停下车，让公主换上一身缀着金饰的华贵白绸裙，又在她颈间和发间戴上珍珠，好让她体体面面地出现在宫廷里。

===Original===
The King and all the council were assembled with great pomp, to greet their new Princess, and all looked grave and anxious. At last the door opened, and Taboret appeared, leading the young girl by the hand.

===Chinese===
国王和全体枢密大臣盛装列队，齐集迎接他们的新公主，个个神情肃穆而焦虑。终于门开了，塔博蕾牵着少女的手走了进来。

===Original===
“That is your father!” said she to Ursula, pointing to the King; and on this, Ursula, needing no other bidding, ran at once to him, and putting her arms round his neck, gave him a sounding kiss.

===Chinese===
“那是你的父亲！”她指着国王对厄休拉说。厄休拉也不用再多吩咐，立刻朝他跑过去，搂住他的脖子，响亮地亲了他一口。

===Original===
His Majesty almost swooned, and all the courtiers shut their eyes and shivered.

===Chinese===
陛下险些晕过去，廷臣们个个闭眼发抖。

===Original===
“This is really!” said one.

===Chinese===
“这可真是的！”一位说。

===Original===
“This is truly!” said another.

===Chinese===
“这可实在是的！”另一位说。

===Original===
“What have I done?” cried Ursula, looking from one to another, and seeing that something was wrong, but not knowing what. “Have I kissed the wrong person?” On hearing which everyone groaned.

===Chinese===
“我做错了什么？”厄休拉喊道，看看这个又看看那个，觉得有点不对，又不知道错在哪里。“难道我亲错了人？”此言一出，众人无不哀叹。

===Original===
“Come now,” cried Taboret, “if you don’t like her, I shall take her away to those who do. I’ll give you a week, and then I’ll come back and see how you’re treating her. She’s a great deal too good for any of you.” So saying she flew away: on her wand, leaving Ursula to get on with her new friends as best she might. But Ursula could not get on with them at all, as she soon began to see.

===Chinese===
“好了，”塔博蕾喊道，“你们不喜欢她，我就带她走，去找喜欢她的人。给你们一个礼拜，到时候我回来看你们怎么待她。她呀，比你们哪一个都好出百倍。”说完她跨上魔杖飞走了，留下厄休拉同这些新朋友尽力相处。可厄休拉很快就发现，她同他们根本处不来。

===Original===
If she spoke or moved they looked shocked, and at last she was so frightened and troubled by them that she burst into tears, at which they were more shocked still.

===Chinese===
她一开口、一动弹，他们就露出惊骇的神色；到后来，她被他们吓得心烦意乱，忍不住放声大哭，他们见了就更加惊骇了。

===Original===
“This is indeed a change after our sweet Princess,” said one lady to another.

===Chinese===
“比起我们那位可人疼的公主来，这可真是天壤之别啊。”一位女官对另一位说。

===Original===
“Yes, indeed,” was the answer, “when one remembers how even after her head was struck off she behaved so beautifully, and only said, ‘Just so.’ ”

===Chinese===
“可不是嘛，”对方答道，“想想看，就连头被敲下来之后，她的仪态还是那样端庄，只说了一声‘正是如此’。”

===Original===
And all the ladies disliked poor Ursula, and soon showed her their dislike. Before the end of the week, when Taboret was to return, she had grown quite thin and pale, and seemed afraid of speaking above a whisper.

===Chinese===
女官们个个讨厌可怜的厄休拉，不多久便把厌烦摆在了脸上。还没到一个礼拜，也就是塔博蕾快回来的时候，她已经变得又瘦又白，说起话来连比耳语高一点的声气都不敢了。

===Original===
“Why, what is wrong?” cried Taboret, when she returned and saw how much poor Ursula had changed. “Don’t you like being here? Aren’t they kind to you?”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，这是怎么了？”塔博蕾回来看见可怜的厄休拉变成这副样子，叫了起来，“你不喜欢住在这儿？他们待你不好吗？”

===Original===
“Take me back, dear Taboret,” cried Ursula, weeping. “Take me back to Oliver, and Philip, and Bell. As for these people, I hate them.”

===Chinese===
“带我回去吧，亲爱的塔博蕾，”厄休拉哭着喊道，“带我回奥利弗、菲利普和贝尔那儿去。至于这些人，我恨他们。”

===Original===
And she wept again.

===Chinese===
说着她又哭了。

===Original===
Taboret only smiled and patted her head, and then went into the King and courtiers.

===Chinese===
塔博蕾只是笑笑，拍拍她的头，随后便到国王和廷臣们那里去了。

===Original===
“Now, how is it,” she cried, “I find the Princess Ursula in tears? and I am sure you are making her unhappy. When you had that bit of wood-and-leather Princess, you could behave well enough to it, but now that you have a real flesh-and-blood woman, you none of you care for her.”

===Chinese===
“喂，这是怎么回事，”她喊道，“我一来就见厄休拉公主在哭？不用问，是你们叫她受了委屈。先前你们有那个木头皮革做的公主，待它倒也够体面的；如今换了有血有肉的真人，你们倒一个个都不把她放在心上了。”

===Original===
“Our late dear daughter﻿—” began the King, when the fairy interrupted him.

===Chinese===
“我们先前那位亲爱的女儿——”国王刚开口，仙女便打断了他。

===Original===
“I do believe,” she said, “that you would like to have the doll back again. Now I will give you your choice. Which will you have﻿—my Princess Ursula, the real one, or your Princess Ursula, the sham?”

===Chinese===
“我算是看明白了，”她说，“你们是想把那布娃娃要回去。好，我让你们自己挑。你们要哪一个——我的厄休拉公主，真的，还是你们的厄休拉公主，假的？”

===Original===
The King sank back into his chair. “I am not equal to this,” he said: “summon the council, and let them settle it by vote.” So the council were summoned, and the fairy explained to them why they were wanted.

===Chinese===
国王颓然坐回椅子里。“这么大的事我裁夺不了，”他说，“召枢密会议来，叫他们投票定夺吧。”于是枢密大臣们被召了来，仙女向他们说明了缘由。

===Original===
“Let both Princesses be fetched,” she said; and the toy Princess was brought in with great care from her cupboard, and her head stood on the table beside her, and the real Princess came in with her eyes still red from crying and her bosom heaving.

===Chinese===
“把两位公主都请来吧。”她说。玩具公主被小心翼翼地从橱柜里捧了出来，头颅端端正正立在桌上她身旁；真公主走了进来，眼睛还哭得通红，胸脯一起一伏。

===Original===
“I should think there could be no doubt which one would prefer,” said the Prime Minister to the Chancellor.

===Chinese===
“我想，该选哪一个，总不至于有什么疑问吧。”首相对宰相说。

===Original===
“I should think not either,” answered the Chancellor.

===Chinese===
“我也想不出会有什么疑问。”宰相答道。

===Original===
“Then vote,” said Taboret; and they all voted, and every vote was for the sham Ursula, and not one for the real one. Taboret only laughed.

===Chinese===
“那就投票吧。”塔博蕾说。于是众人投票，票票都投给假的厄休拉，没有一票投给真的。塔博蕾只是笑。

===Original===
“You are a pack of sillies and idiots,” she said, “but you shall have what you want;” and she picked up the head, and with a wave of her wand stuck it on to the body, and it moved round slowly and said, “Certainly,” just in its old voice; and on hearing this, all the courtiers gave something as like a cheer as they thought polite, whilst the old King could not speak for joy.

===Chinese===
“你们是一帮傻子和笨蛋，”她说，“可你们想要什么，就叫你们得什么。”说罢她拾起那头颅，魔杖一挥，把它安回身子上。头颅缓缓转了一圈，用它从前的嗓音说了声“当然”。廷臣们一听，都发出了自认为不失体面的欢呼，老国王更是欢喜得说不出话来。

===Original===
“We will,” he cried, “at once make our arrangements for abdicating and leaving the government in the hands of our dear daughter;” and on hearing this the courtiers all applauded again.

===Chinese===
“我们即刻安排逊位，”他喊道，“把国政交到我们亲爱的女儿手里。”廷臣们听了，又齐声喝彩。

===Original===
But Taboret laughed scornfully, and taking up the real Ursula in her arms, flew back with her to Mark’s cottage.

===Chinese===
塔博蕾却冷冷一笑，抱起真正的厄休拉，带着她飞回马克家的小屋去了。

===Original===
In the evening the city was illuminated, and there were great rejoicings at the recovery of the Princess, but Ursula remained in the cottage and married Oliver, and lived happily with him for the rest of her life.

===Chinese===
当天晚上，城里张灯结彩，为公主失而复得举行盛大的欢庆；可厄休拉留在了小渔屋里，嫁给了奥利弗，同他幸福地过了一辈子。

## On a Pincushion / 针插之上

===Original===
On a pincushion were a pebble Brooch, a jet Shawl-pin, and a common Pin. They were all complaining because they were so often left behind instead of being taken out like the other brooches and pins.

===Chinese===
针插上插着一枚卵石胸针（pebble Brooch）、一枚煤玉披肩别针（jet Shawl-pin），还有一枚普普通通的大头针（common Pin）。它们三个都在发牢骚：别的胸针别针常常被取出佩戴，它们却总是被留在家里。

===Original===
“It is all very well for you,” said the Shawl-pin, “but for me it is trying, because I have seen better days, and remember the time when I was never used to pin up anything but the finest Indian and Cashmere shawls.”

===Chinese===
“你们倒没什么，”披肩别针说，“我却是煎熬，想当年我也风光过，记得那时候，除了最上等的印度披肩和开司米披肩，我从没被拿来别过别的。”

===Original===
“Nay,” cried the Brooch, “you cannot expect to be used as much as I, for you are all black, and would not be taken for anything but a dark shawl. But I am all sorts of colours, and therefore might be used any day. I would sooner have been left uncut, unpolished, than brought to this.”

===Chinese===
“不然，”胸针嚷道，“你别指望能像我这样常被用上，你一身乌黑，除了深色披肩，再不会有人拿你配别的。可我什么颜色都有，天天都用得着。我宁可当初不被切割、不被打磨，也不愿落到今天这步田地。”

===Original===
“I don’t think,” said the Pin, “that either of you have as much cause to complain as I, for you are neither of you as useful, and might not be wanted, but I am always needed, and so many pins are taken every day that it seems hard I should be left here for nearly a week, and all because I am run so far into the pincushion that nothing but my head can be seen.”

===Chinese===
“依我看哪，”大头针说，“你们俩谁也比不上我有得抱怨：你们俩都不怎么顶用，人家少了你们也罢了；我可是一天也缺不得的，每天取用的大头针那么多，偏偏我被丢在这儿快一个礼拜了，全都因为我扎进针插太深，只露着一个针头。”

===Original===
After a pause the Shawl-pin said, “I wish those Bracelets up there would leave off chattering. There’s nothing disturbs my nerves so much as the clatter of talking.”

===Chinese===
停了半晌，披肩别针说：“但愿上头那几只手镯（Bracelets）别再叽叽呱呱了。天底下再没有比说话的嘈杂声更搅扰我神经的东西了。”

===Original===
“Bracelets are always great talkers,” said the Brooch. “I once passed two months in a jewel-box with a number, and I was truly thankful when I was taken out. Their talking was incessant, and it was impossible to get a wink of sleep.” And all three scowled up at the Bracelets, who were hanging over the looking-glass, but who did not mind them in the least, but went on talking just the same. “Let us do something to drown their noise,” said the Pin. “Let us tell stories.”

===Chinese===
“手镯向来是顶爱说话的，”胸针说，“有一回我跟好几只手镯在一只首饰盒里待了两个月，给取出来的时候，我真是谢天谢地。她们说个没完没了，叫人连个盹都打不成。”三个都朝手镯们瞪眼——手镯们悬在镜子上方，却半点不理会，照旧说个不停。“咱们干点什么，压一压她们的吵闹吧，”大头针说，“讲故事怎么样？”

===Original===
“I will tell you one,” said the Brooch, “and I know it to be true, for it was before I was cut and polished, and I was at the place myself where it happened;” and having cleared its throat, the Brooch began as follows.

===Chinese===
“我来讲一个，”胸针说，“这故事我知道千真万确，因为那时我还没被切割、没被打磨，事情发生的地方，我本人就在场。”说罢清了清嗓子，胸针便这样讲了起来。

## Siegfrid and Handa / 齐格弗里德与汉达

===Original===
On the border of a large forest there once stood a little village, where all the people were happy, for they were all good and industrious and honest. It was the pleasantest little village in the world. No fevers or illnesses ever came near it. The people died only of old age, and all the children were well grown and strong. The villagers never quarrelled with each other, but lived as peacefully as the flowers in the forest. Now, there was an old proverb about the place, which said that as long as the people were honest and hardworking, and never quarrelled or were greedy or avaricious, no troubles should come near them, but that heavy misfortune would fall on them directly they forgot to behave well. The children in this village were as happy as their parents, but perhaps the happiest child of all was little Siegfrid, the shoemaker’s little son. He had no brothers or sisters, but he never felt lonely, for he played with the miller’s little daughter Handa, who was just his own age, and as pretty a little maiden as you would wish to see. Siegfrid’s father, Ralph, was the only shoemaker in the village, and made shoes and boots for all the people. Once a year each one would come to him and say, “Ralph, I want a new pair of shoes, and be sure you make them as good as the last.” So Ralph had plenty to do, but he took a long time making each pair, for he always wanted them to be well made, and would have been ashamed if he had been told that they had worn out before their time or did not fit well.

===Chinese===
从前，在一片大森林的边上有一座小村庄，那里人人幸福，因为个个善良、勤劳、诚实。那是世上最安乐的一座小村庄。热病和瘟疫从不挨近它，村里人都是寿终而逝，孩子们个个长得壮实。村民彼此从不争吵，像林中的花儿一样和和美美地过日子。关于这地方有句古老的谚语，说只要人们诚实勤勉，不争吵、不贪心、不贪财，灾祸就不会挨近他们；可一旦他们忘了本分，大难便会立刻临头。村里的孩子们同父母一样快活，而其中最快活的，恐怕要数鞋匠家的小儿子齐格弗里德（Siegfrid）了。他没有兄弟姐妹，却从不觉得孤单，因为他总和磨坊主家的小女儿汉达（Handa）一起玩，汉达同他正好同岁，是个你再也寻不出第二个的漂亮小姑娘。齐格弗里德的父亲拉尔夫（Ralph）是村里唯一的鞋匠，全村人的鞋子靴子都出自他的手。一年一回，每个人都会来找他说：“拉尔夫，我要一双新鞋，你可要做同上一双一样结实。”所以拉尔夫活计不少，可他做每一双鞋都费好些时日，因为他总要把鞋做得尽善尽美；要是听人说他的鞋不耐穿、不合脚，他会羞臊得无地自容。

===Original===
His father meant to teach little Siegfrid his trade, and that he should be village shoemaker after him; and when he was grown up Siegfrid meant to marry Handa, and already the villagers, when they saw the children walking hand in hand to and from school, as they did every day, laughed and said, “There go Siegfrid and his little wife.”

===Chinese===
父亲打算把手艺教给小齐格弗里德，好让他日后接自己做村里的鞋匠；齐格弗里德呢，也打定主意长大后娶汉达为妻。两个孩子天天手拉手上学、放学，村人见了就笑着说：“瞧，齐格弗里德和他的小媳妇儿走过去了。”

===Original===
The forest near was full of birds and animals, and as the villagers never hunted or hurt them in any way, they had grown quite tame. The birds would fly down from the branches and sit upon the shoulders of the passersby, or flutter round their heads singing. The squirrels sat still and let anyone stroke them, and even the timid hares came and gently rubbed themselves against the children’s legs and feet, with no more fear of them than a pussy cat would have had; in fact, the animals in the forest were as peaceful and happy as the men and women in the village.

===Chinese===
近旁的森林里满是飞禽走兽，因为村人从不猎捕伤害它们，它们都变得十分驯顺。鸟儿会从枝头飞下来，落在过路人的肩头，或绕着他们的脑袋飞舞歌唱。松鼠安安静静蹲着，任谁抚摸；连胆小的野兔也会跑来，在孩子腿边脚边轻轻蹭痒，一点也不怕他们，就像家猫那样不怕；说实在的，林中的走兽同村里的人们一样太平安乐。

===Original===
One day there came along the high road a queer little old man﻿—an ugly-looking man﻿—with small eyes and a peaked cap on his head. On his back he carried a heavy pack like a pedlar’s. When he got into the village he stopped and began to undo it, whilst all the children stood round him staring. Everyone who passed stopped and looked at him, for it was very unusual for any stranger to come to the village. Then the old man took some poles and a board, which he had carried on his back also, and with them he made a stall, which he placed by the roadside. Then he undid his pack and took from it a number of boots and shoes, which he placed on the stall, and then sat down himself on a little stool behind it.

===Chinese===
一天，大路上来了一个古怪的小老头——相貌丑陋——一双小眼睛，头上戴一顶尖顶帽，背上背着个货郎用的大包袱。他进了村子便停下，动手解那包袱，孩子们全都围着他瞪眼瞧。过路的人也个个停下来看他，因为村里难得有外乡人来。接着老头取出几根竿子和一块木板——也是一路背来的——搭起一个货摊，摆在路边。然后他解开包袱，从里面掏出一双双靴子鞋子摆上摊子，自己坐在摊后的小凳上。

===Original===
All the children gathered round the stall to look, for never had they seen such a number of boots and shoes together before. There were big ones and little ones, blue, green, red, yellow, violet, every colour﻿—some with bows and rosettes, others quite plain. And all had their prices marked on them; and oh! how cheap they were! The boots and shoes Ralph made cost three times as much.

===Chinese===
孩子们全都围到摊子边看，他们从没见过这么多靴子鞋子摆在一块儿。大的小的，蓝的绿的，红的黄的，紫的，什么颜色都有——有的缀着蝴蝶结和花结，有的素净得很。每双都标着价钱；哎呀，真便宜！拉尔夫做的靴子鞋子，价钱得是这的三倍。

===Original===
After a time the old man began to sing:﻿—

===Chinese===
过了一会儿，老头唱了起来：——

===Original===
> “Come, buy! come, buy!
 

 Shoes for all.
 

 Who’ll try? who’ll try?”
> “Red shoes and blue shoes,
 

 Black shoes and white shoes,
 

 Thick shoes and thin shoes,
 

 Strong shoes and light shoes.”

===Chinese===
> “来买呀！来买呀！
 

 鞋子人人有。
 

 谁来试？谁来试？”
> “红鞋子，蓝鞋子，
 

 黑鞋子，白鞋子，
 

 厚鞋子，薄鞋子，
 

 结实鞋，轻便鞋。”

===Original===
But the villagers all passed on, and only laughed as they looked back at the stall, and said, “We don’t want him here. We have Ralph to make our boots and shoes, and he does quite well for us.” So no customers came to the old man that day, and when night came he quietly packed up his wares and took his stall to pieces, and went his way. But the first thing next morning there he was again putting up his stall and covering it with boots and shoes. Every day for many weeks he came and sat by the roadside, in exactly the same spot, singing the same rhyme, and still no one went near him to buy his goods, and the children grew so used to him that they no longer stopped to stare at him.

===Chinese===
可村人只是走过，回头望望那货摊，笑笑说：“咱们用不着他。咱们有拉尔夫做靴子鞋子，他做的尽够咱们穿。”所以那天老头一个主顾也没等到。天黑了，他不声不响把货色收好，拆了摊子走了。可第二天一早，他又在老地方支起摊子，摆满靴子鞋子。一连许多个礼拜，他天天来坐在路边，坐在分毫不差的同一处，唱着同一支歌谣，还是没有人上前买他的货；孩子们见惯了，也不再停下来瞪着他瞧了。

===Original===
But one day a young girl named Lisbeth, the daughter of the baker, came near the stall, and looked at a pair of red shoes, with pretty buckles, and the old man looked at her, but did not say a word. Then she turned away, but she felt the money in her pocket as she walked, and she looked back at the shoes, and the old man nodded and chuckled to himself as he watched her. Next morning she came again, but this time she took up the shoes in her hand, and examined them well; she turned them about a little, and then she put them down and walked away as before; but in the evening she came back, and took them up again, and this time she did not lay them down, but took out her money, and paid the old man for them, and then ran away, hiding the shoes under her apron, for she was rather afraid of being laughed at for having bought shoes of the old man. At once she took them to her greatest friend, who was a girl named Alys. “Look at these pretty pink shoes,” she said. “I have just bought them of the old man at the stall, and they only cost five silver pennies. See how pretty they are!” Alys looked at them and said, “It is true they are very pretty, but I don’t think they will wear well; however, as they are so very cheap, that does not much matter. I wonder if he has another pair just like them, that I could buy.”

===Chinese===
可是有一天，面包师的女儿、名叫莉丝贝特（Lisbeth）的姑娘走近了摊子，看中一双缀着漂亮带扣的红鞋。老头望了望她，一句话也不说。她转身走开了，可一路走一路摸着口袋里的钱，又回头望那双鞋；老头看着她，点了点头，独自嘿嘿地笑。第二天早晨她又来了，这回把鞋拿在手里细细端详，翻来覆去看了几遍，又放下走开了，同先前一样；可到了傍晚她又回来，重新拿起那双鞋，这一回她没有再放下，而是掏出钱付给老头，然后抱着鞋跑了，把鞋藏在围裙底下，因为她有点怕人笑话她买了老头的鞋。她径直拿去给她最要好的朋友、一个名叫阿莉丝（Alys）的姑娘看。“瞧瞧这双粉红鞋多漂亮，”她说，“我刚从摊上老头那儿买的，才花了五枚银便士。你看看多好看！”阿莉丝看了看，说：“确实很漂亮，不过我怕它不耐穿；好在这么便宜，倒也无所谓。不知道他那儿还有没有一模一样的一双，我也好买一双。”

===Original===
So Lisbeth and Alys went back to the stall, and Alys bought a pair of shoes just like Lisbeth’s; and then they both went to show them to the other young girls in the village, and one by one each said she would have a pair for herself, till there was not a girl who was not wearing the old man’s shoes. Then the women began. If they were so cheap, they said, why should they not buy them too? They were not bound to buy only Ralph’s goods. So after a time all the women had bought boots for themselves, and shoes for their little ones, from the old man, and then they began to persuade their husbands to go and buy of him also. At first all the men refused, saying that Ralph had made their boots all his life, and it was unfair to give him up now; but the women went on persuading, and one by one the men consented; till at last there was no one in the village, except Ralph and his son, little Siegfrid, who was not wearing the old man’s boots and shoes. At first Ralph only laughed, and said that they would soon come back to his little shop when they found what the old man’s boots were worth, for no one could sell good things at such a price. But the boots wore out, and still all the people went back again to the old man’s stall for more, though they knew that they would not last for long.

===Chinese===
于是莉丝贝特和阿莉丝回到摊上，阿莉丝买了一双同莉丝贝特一模一样的鞋；随后两人拿去给村里别的姑娘们看，姑娘们一个接一个都说自己也要买一双，到后来，没有一个姑娘脚上不穿着老头的鞋了。接着女人们也动心了。她们说，既然这么便宜，为什么自己不能买？又没人规定只许买拉尔夫的货。于是过了些日子，全村的女人都给自己从老头那儿买了靴子，给孩子买了鞋子，然后又劝各自的丈夫也去买。起初男人们都不肯，说拉尔夫给他们做了一辈子靴子，如今撇下他实在不该；可女人们劝个不停，男人们便一个接一个松了口；到最后，全村除了拉尔夫和他的儿子小齐格弗里德，再没有一个人不是穿着老头的靴鞋了。起初拉尔夫只是笑笑，说等大家明白老头的靴子是什么货色，自然还会回他这爿小店来，因为谁也不可能按那个价钱卖出好东西来。可靴子穿破了，人们还是照样回到老头的摊上去买，明知道穿不了多久也照买不误。

===Original===
Then Ralph looked very grave, and began to say to everyone that if they did not change, and return to buy his boots and shoes, as before, he would have to leave the village and go elsewhere, where his goods would find customers, for he could not live there if he sold nothing.

===Chinese===
于是拉尔夫脸色沉重起来，逢人便说，要是大家不改一改，不像从前那样回来买他的靴鞋，他只好离开村子，另投别处——总有个地方他的货卖得出去——因为一件东西都卖不掉，他在村里是活不下去的。

===Original===
But strange to say, just at this time, though the spring was well advanced, there was one night a bitter black frost, and in the morning the farmers found that all the young green corn was nipped and killed. Such a thing had never been known to happen before, and much it frightened the people, for what would they do if they had no corn with which to make their bread?

===Chinese===
可是说来也怪，正在这时候，明明春天已经过了大半，一天夜里却降了一场酷烈的黑霜，第二天早晨，庄稼人发现青嫩的庄稼全都冻死了。这样的事从来没有听说过，人们吓得不轻，因为没有麦子磨面做面包，他们可怎么活呢？

===Original===
Then there fell over everything a terrible blight, and it killed all the young fruit, which fell from the trees. This alarmed the people even more, for it would indeed be bad if they had neither fruit nor bread to eat.

===Chinese===
接着一场可怕的枯萎病罩了下来，把刚结的果子全都毁了，果实纷纷从树上掉落。这更叫人心慌，因为要是果子面包都没得吃，那日子可真没法过了。

===Original===
Then the weather turned very hot﻿—so hot that no one could endure it﻿—and all the grass was dried up, and the cows had nothing to eat, so that there was no milk to be had; and then the wells and streams began to dry up, and the people began to fear there would soon be no water either. The rains did not come, and then, worst of all, a bad fever broke out in the village, and many persons fell sick. Still, no one thought that all these misfortunes had anything to do with the old shoemaker, who still sat by the roadside, selling his shoes and singing his queer rhymes. At last, one day, one of the men remembered the old saying, that the people of that village should always be prosperous as long as they were honest and industrious, and neither cruel nor greedy.

===Chinese===
随后天气又变得酷热——热得谁都受不住——青草全都枯了，奶牛没了吃的，牛奶也就断了；接着井水溪水开始干涸，人们又怕起连水也快没有了。雨迟迟不下，最糟的是，村里又爆发了一场恶性热病，许多人都病倒了。尽管如此，还是没有人想到，这一切灾祸同那个老鞋匠有什么相干——他仍旧坐在路边卖他的鞋，唱他那古怪的歌谣。终于有一天，有个汉子想起了那句老话：这村子的人只要诚实勤勉、不刻薄不贪婪，村子就能永远兴旺。

===Original===
“But it is not our conduct,” said they, “which has brought all this trouble on us, for we have been neither cruel, nor dishonest, nor greedy.”

===Chinese===
“可招来这些祸患的并不是我们的品行，”他们说，“因为我们既不刻薄，也不奸猾，更不贪心。”

===Original===
But little Handa shook her head and said, “You are very cruel, you are letting poor Ralph starve, because you will all buy your boots and shoes of the new old man. Ralph has worked well for you all his life, and now you all leave him and buy boots from the old man, just because he is new, though you know they are not half as good as Ralph’s.”

===Chinese===
可小汉达摇了摇头，说：“你们就是刻薄。你们眼看着可怜的拉尔夫挨饿，就因为你们都去买那个新来的老头的靴鞋。拉尔夫给你们做了一辈子的好活计，如今你们全都撇下他，去老头那儿买靴子，就因为他是个新面孔，尽管你们明知道他的靴子连拉尔夫的一半都比不上。”

===Original===
“You are talking of what you don’t understand, Handa,” said her father, the miller, angrily. “Of course it is right for us to buy everything as cheaply as we can. Be quiet, child!” And everyone was angry with Handa for speaking; but she thought just the same, and she cried when she saw Ralph, or his wife, or Siegfrid.

===Chinese===
“汉达，小孩子家懂什么。”她父亲磨坊主生气地说，“东西当然越便宜买得越对。住嘴，孩子！”大家都怪汉达多嘴；可她心里还是那么想，一看见拉尔夫，或是拉尔夫的妻子，或是齐格弗里德，就要掉眼泪。

===Original===
The fever in the village spread, and Ralph caught it, and was very ill, and had to lie in bed all day. He could not now have made boots, if anyone had come to buy them of him. At last he and his wife grew so poor that she had to sell all their furniture, to buy bread for them to eat. When that was gone she began to sell all the clothes they could do without, even to the boots from their feet, so that little Siegfrid and she had to go about barefoot. The famine in the village grew worse, and the men began to trap and kill the animals in the forest to eat. At last there came a worse misfortune than all. A little girl named Frieda, the daughter of a farmer, disappeared, and could not be found anywhere. They sought for her far and near, in the village and in the forest, but she was nowhere to be found, and everyone made up their minds that she had been stolen. But next day another little girl was missing, and it was vain to search for either. The next day another went, and then another, and another, till five little girls had disappeared; and on the sixth day little Handa went also. Then the people all sallied forth in a body, with swords and sticks; and they walked for miles around the village, and sought in every corner of the forest, but no trace of the children could be seen. Still, Handa’s father would not rest, but walked about looking for her both day and night, whilst poor Siegfrid sought till his feet were blistered, and cut, and he was so weary that he could go no farther.

===Chinese===
村里的热病蔓延开来，拉尔夫也染上了，病得很重，整日躺在床上。这会儿就算有人来买靴子，他也做不动了。到后来两口子穷到了底，妻子只好把家具统统卖掉换面包吃。家具卖光了，她又开始卖一切能省下的衣裳，连脚上的靴子也卖了，小齐格弗里德和她只好赤着脚走路。村里的饥荒越来越重，男人们开始在林子里设陷阱捕杀野兽充饥。最后，来了一场比先前所有灾祸都重的祸事。一个庄稼人的小女儿、名叫弗丽达（Frieda）的小姑娘失踪了，哪里都找不见。他们在村里村外、林里林外寻了个遍，可哪儿也没有她的影儿，大家都认定她是被人偷走了。可第二天又丢了一个小姑娘，两个人都白找了一场。第三天又丢一个，接着又一个、又一个，一共五个小姑娘不见了；第六天，小汉达也不见了。于是全村的人带着刀剑棍棒，成群结队地出动，把村子方圆多少里的地方都走遍了，森林的每个角落都搜遍了，却看不见孩子们的一点踪迹。汉达的父亲还是不肯歇，日夜奔走寻她；可怜的齐格弗里德也一直找啊找，脚上磨出了泡、割破了口，累得再也走不动一步。

===Original===
No one, who had known it in its old days, would have recognized the little village again. Instead of the villagers looking healthy, and happy, and rosy, they were worn, and sad, and pale, whilst the women’s eyes were red with crying for the lost children. The houses were tumbling down, and none of the people seemed to have strength or care to build them up again.

===Chinese===
认识这村子旧日模样的人，如今再也认不出它了。村人从前那样健康、快活、红光满面，如今却个个憔悴、悲哀、面色苍白，女人们的眼睛都为丢失的孩子哭红了。房屋东倒西塌，却似乎谁也没有力气、也没有心思再修。

===Original===
Over all the place hung a hot thick mist, and each day the fever grew worse, and more were ill.

===Chinese===
整个地方笼罩着一层又热又浓的迷雾，热病一天比一天重，病倒的人一天比一天多。

===Original===
The second evening after Handa had disappeared, Siegfrid wandered into the forest to cry by himself. Even that was changed; no birds sang sweetly in the branches as of yore. The leaves on the trees were turning brown, and falling before their time, and the animals darted away at the sound of footsteps, afraid lest they might be caught and killed.

===Chinese===
汉达失踪后的第二天傍晚，齐格弗里德独自溜进森林里去哭。连森林也变了样：枝头不再有鸟儿像往日那样婉转啼唱。树上的叶子泛黄转枯，没到时候就纷纷飘落，走兽一听见脚步声便窜得远远的，生怕被人捉住杀掉。

===Original===
As Siegfrid walked along he kicked something with his foot, and found it was a trap in which a poor little Hare had been caught, and was held by one leg.

===Chinese===
齐格弗里德走着走着，脚碰到了一样东西，低头一看，是个捕兽夹，夹住了一只可怜的小野兔，一条腿被紧紧夹着。

===Original===
“Poor Hare,” said Siegfrid, “perhaps you used to play about us when I walked here with Handa, I will let you go, and then another time you will be careful not to be caught;” so he undid the trap, and the Hare sprang from it, but instead of running away, as Siegfrid had expected, it sat quite still in front of him, looking into his face.

===Chinese===
“可怜的野兔，”齐格弗里德说，“从前我和汉达在这儿走的时候，说不定你还绕着我们玩儿呢。我放你走，往后你可要小心，别再被夹住了。”说着他掰开捕兽夹，野兔一跃而出，却没有像齐格弗里德想的那样跑掉，而是端端正正蹲在他面前，望着他的脸。

===Original===
“I saw Handa last night,” it said at last, in a wheezy voice.

===Chinese===
“我昨天夜里看见汉达了。”它终于开口了，声音喘喘的。

===Original===
Siegfrid stared, but he was so overjoyed at hearing again of Handa, that he quite got over his surprise at hearing a Hare speak.

===Chinese===
齐格弗里德愣住了，可是再次听到汉达的消息，他实在太欢喜，听见野兔说话的那点惊诧，早飞到九霄云外去了。

===Original===
“Saw Handa!” he cried. “Oh, where? Is she alive? Oh, tell me.”

===Chinese===
“看见汉达了！”他喊道，“啊，在哪儿？她还活着吗？快告诉我！”

===Original===
“She is in a cavern underground,” said the Hare. “She and all the other little girls are sitting there in a row, and they cannot move or speak, because on their feet are magic shoes that the old shoemaker made for them, which hold them as still as marble. He waited for them one by one near the village, and gave to each a pair of pretty yellow shoes, and when she had put them on, they ran away with her, and she could not stop try what she might; and the shoes took her right into the middle of the forest. Then the ground opened, and the shoes ran right down into the cavern underground, and the earth closed up again; and there sit poor Handa and the other five little girls, who were all brought there in the same way; and they will never move till someone pulls the shoes from their feet. Now the old man is waiting till tonight, when he will steal another little girl, and that will make seven, and then he will take them away and kill them. In reality he is a kind of gnome, and very cruel and wicked. He has disguised himself as a shoemaker, that he may steal these little girls. He belongs to a kind which only lives three hundred years, and then they begin to grow small, and gradually become less and less, till at last they vanish quite into air, unless they can steal seven little girls from whose bones they make a dreadful charm, which gives them life for another hundred years. I cannot tell you how many children this old man has killed, for he is nearly two thousand years old; but now the time has come for him to kill seven more children, and if he cannot do it, he will shrivel and shrink, and at last vanish away.”

===Chinese===
“她在一处地下的洞穴里，”野兔说，“她同其余的小姑娘一排坐在那儿，动也动不得，说也说不出，因为她们脚上都穿着老鞋匠做的魔法鞋，那鞋把她们定得像大理石像一样。他在村子附近一个一个地候着她们，送给每人一双漂亮的黄鞋；等她们一穿上，那鞋就带着她们跑，怎么也停不下；鞋子一直把她们带到密林深处，然后地面裂开，鞋子径直跑进地下的洞穴，泥土又合拢起来。可怜的汉达同另外五个小姑娘就坐在那儿，全都是这样被带进去的；不把鞋从她们脚上扒下来，她们永远动不了。眼下那老头正等着今晚，还要再偷一个小姑娘，凑足七个，然后他就把她们带走杀掉。其实他是种地精（gnome），又狠毒又邪恶。他扮成鞋匠的样子，就为了偷这些小姑娘。他那一类地精只能活三百年，之后便开始缩小，越缩越小，最后彻底化进空气里；除非他们能偷到七个小姑娘，用她们的骨头做一道可怕的魔咒，好再换一百年阳寿。这老头杀过多少孩子，我说不清，他差不多有两千岁了；如今又到了他要再杀七个孩子的日子，要是办不成，他就会干瘪、缩小，最后彻底消散。”

===Original===
Then Siegfrid clapped his hands. “That will be capital!” he cried. “I will go and pull the shoes off their feet at once, and then when he comes he will find them gone, and he will not be able to get more in time, so that he will shrink and vanish.”

===Chinese===
齐格弗里德一听便拍起手来。“那可太好了！”他喊道，“我这就去把鞋从她们脚上扒下来，等他来的时候，人已经不见了，他来不及再偷，就会缩小、消散！”

===Original===
The Hare shook its head. “You are going too fast,” it said. “The shoes cannot be drawn from the children’s feet as long as a single person in the village is wearing the boots and shoes made by the old man. So the first thing you must do will be to get all the shoes from everyone in the village, and put them all into a heap, and burn them all together.”

===Chinese===
野兔摇摇头。“你想得太容易了，”它说，“只要村里还有一个人穿着老头做的靴鞋，那鞋就没法从孩子们脚上脱下来。所以你第一件要办的事，就是把全村人的鞋都收拢来，堆作一堆，一并烧掉。”

===Original===
“But how can I do that?” asked Siegfrid. “The people will never give me their boots to burn, and if I tell them all you tell me they will not believe me.”

===Chinese===
“可这我怎么办得到呢？”齐格弗里德问，“人们绝不会把靴子给我来烧，我把你说的这些讲给他们听，他们也不会信。”

===Original===
“Let us go and ask the Owl,” said the Hare. “He flies about at night when there is no one to see. Perhaps he would steal them for you and bring them here.” So Siegfrid and the Hare started off together to find the Owl. He was sitting on a bough half asleep, for it was yet light, so he had not quite waked up, and Siegfrid had to throw a bit of wood at him to wake him. He looked very cross when he opened his eyes and heard what they wanted, but he said﻿—

===Chinese===
“咱们去找猫头鹰问问吧，”野兔说，“他夜里飞来飞去，没人瞧得见，说不定他肯替你把鞋偷来，送到这儿。”于是齐格弗里德和野兔一同动身去找猫头鹰。猫头鹰正蹲在枝头打盹，因为天还没黑，他没完全醒，齐格弗里德只好扔了块小木头把他砸醒。他睁开眼听了他们的来意，一脸的不痛快，可他还是说——

===Original===
“I will do it for you if you will pay me. If you will give me one of your eyes, I will bring you everyone’s boots from the village tonight, after they are all in bed.”

===Chinese===
“替你办事可以，你得给我报酬。你要是肯把你一只眼睛给我，今夜等全村人都睡下，我就把每个人的靴子都给你取来。”

===Original===
“But why do you want my eye?” said Siegfrid. “It can be of no use to you. You will not be able to see with my eye; and perhaps Handa will not love me if I have only one eye.”

===Chinese===
“可你要我的眼睛做什么呢？”齐格弗里德说，“那对你毫无用处，你用我的眼睛是看不见东西的；再说我只剩一只眼睛，汉达也许就不爱我了。”

===Original===
“That’s not my business,” said the Owl. “I want your eye, and I will not get you the shoes without it. I am very blind, and I think I might see better with your eye than I do with my own.”

===Chinese===
“那可不关我的事，”猫头鹰说，“我要你的眼睛，不给就别想拿到鞋。我眼神很不好，我琢磨着，换上你的眼睛，兴许比我自己的还看得清楚些。”

===Original===
“Very well, then,” said Siegfrid, sighing. “Bring me the boots and shoes, and you shall have what you want.”

===Chinese===
“那么好吧，”齐格弗里德叹了口气说，“你把靴鞋取来，你要什么就给你什么。”

===Original===
“Come back at twelve tonight to this same place, and you will find all the boots and shoes here,” croaked the Owl, and then he went to sleep again, and Siegfrid wandered about the forest alone, for the Hare soon scampered away, saying she would be back at night. But Siegfrid would not go home for fear he should be kept there, and not allowed to go back to the forest.

===Chinese===
“今夜十二点，你回到这个地方来，靴子鞋子都会在这儿等你。”猫头鹰哑着嗓子说完，又睡去了。齐格弗里德独自在林子里转悠，野兔说它夜里再来，蹦蹦跳跳地先走了。齐格弗里德却不敢回家，生怕家里人把他留住，不放他回森林来。

===Original===
He cried at the thought of losing one of his bright brown eyes. “But I would give both my eyes for Handa,” he said to himself, “only then I should not be able to see her at all.”

===Chinese===
一想到要失去一只明亮的棕色眼睛，他就哭了。“可为了汉达，两只眼睛我都舍得，”他自言自语道，“只是那样一来，我就根本看不见她了。”

===Original===
The evening wore away, and when twelve﻿—o’clock came, he went back to the place where he had left the Owl, and there, on a large bare spot under the trees, were heaped in a pile all the boots and shoes that the old man had sold since he came to the village.

===Chinese===
黑夜慢慢过去，十二点钟一到，他便回到同猫头鹰分手的地方。只见树下一大块空地上，堆着小山似的一堆，老头进村以来卖出的靴子鞋子，一双不落全在这儿了。

===Original===
Most were nearly worn out; some, indeed, were nothing more than old soles with little bits of leather hanging to them; but there they all were. There was the pretty pair of blue shoes that had first tempted Lisbeth, with the rosette torn off and worn quite white, and with great holes in the sides, and there also was the pair which Alys had bought like them.

===Chinese===
大多数都快穿烂了，有几双干脆只剩旧鞋底，挂着几条破皮子；可它们一双双全在这儿。先勾住莉丝贝特的那双漂亮蓝鞋也在——花结扯掉了，磨得发白，侧面破了大洞——阿莉丝照着买的那双也在。

===Original===
On one side of the heap was the Owl, and the Hare sat beside him.

===Chinese===
鞋堆一旁站着猫头鹰，野兔蹲在他身边。

===Original===
“There they all are,” said the Owl. “Now let me be paid.”

===Chinese===
“全在这儿了，”猫头鹰说，“现在给我报酬吧。”

===Original===
Siegfrid sighed, but he would not break his promise; so he took out his right eye and gave it to the Owl, who flew away with it, hooting in triumph.

===Chinese===
齐格弗里德叹了口气，可他不愿食言，便取出右眼，递给了猫头鹰。猫头鹰带着它飞走了，得意地呜呜叫着。

===Original===
“I hope Handa will know me,” he thought; and he felt almost inclined to cry, but he would not let the Hare see it, so he only said﻿—

===Chinese===
“但愿汉达还认得我。”他想道。他几乎要哭出来，却不愿让野兔看见，只是说——

===Original===
“Let us set fire to them at once, then.”

===Chinese===
“那咱们这就放火烧了吧。”

===Original===
“First listen to me,” said the Hare, “for when the shoes begin to burn I shall run off. When they have quite burnt out, you will find a heap of black ashes, and amongst them you must search carefully, and you will find a pair of sandals that seem not the least burnt or singed. These you must put on, and then stamp hard upon the ground, and it will open, and in front of you, you will see a long dark passage which leads to the underground cavern in which sit Handa and her companions. The sandals will last till you again step out of the passage into the forest, and then they will crumble and fall to pieces as if they had been burnt. So now goodbye. I should not have told you if you had not let me out of the trap.”

===Chinese===
“先听我说，”野兔说，“鞋一烧起来我就得跑了。等火烧尽了，你会看到一堆黑灰，你要在灰里细细地找，会找到一双凉鞋，一点没烧着，也没燎焦。你要穿上它，然后朝地上使劲跺脚，地面就会裂开，你面前会出现一条长长的黑通道，直通汉达和她的伙伴们坐着的地底洞穴。这双凉鞋能撑到你再走出通道、回到森林的时候，然后它就会碎裂、散掉，就像被火烧过似的。好了，再见。要不是你把我从捕兽夹里放出来，我是不会告诉你这些的。”

===Original===
“Goodbye, kind Hare, and thank you for telling me,” said Siegfrid, as the Hare ran off.

===Chinese===
“再见，好心的野兔，谢谢你告诉我。”野兔跑开时，齐格弗里德说道。

===Original===
Then he took a bit of dry wood and set it alight, and placed it under the pile of boots. They soon caught fire, and burnt steadily, but Siegfrid noticed that they made no noise except a low hissing like water boiling. The pile went on burning for some time, and then went out quite suddenly, with a loud bang, leaving no sparks or smoke, and when Siegfrid felt among the ashes he found they were quite cold. He searched among them as the Hare had directed, and at the bottom of the heap he found a pair of sandals, which seemed to be quite unhurt by the fire. He drew them on to his feet, and then standing in the middle of the ashes stamped hard. At once he felt the earth begin to move, and a great hole opened in front of him, which appeared to be the beginning of a long dark passage.

===Chinese===
于是他拾了块干木头点着，塞到靴堆底下。靴子很快烧着了，稳稳地烧着，齐格弗里德却注意到，火里没什么声响，只有低低的嘶嘶声，像水开了锅。那堆东西烧了好一阵，忽然“砰”的一声熄了，火星烟尘一点没有；齐格弗里德伸手到灰里一摸，灰竟是凉的。他照野兔嘱咐的在灰里细细翻找，在灰堆底下找到一双凉鞋，看样子一点没被火伤着。他把凉鞋穿上脚，站在灰堆正中使劲一跺。立时他觉出大地动了起来，面前裂开一个大洞，看样子是一条长长的黑通道的入口。

===Original===
Siegfrid summoned all his courage, and sitting on the edge of the hole dropped gently into the passage, and ran down it. It was quite dark, and so narrow that if he moved either of his hands he knocked the walls, and if he stretched his neck he touched the ceiling. There was only just room for him to walk, so that there was no fear of his losing his way. He went on and on, and still there was no light or no opening to right or to left. Then he began to call. He called “Handa, Handa, Handa!” and the name rang back to him from every side, but he knew it was not Handa’s voice he heard, but only a mocking echo. He feared the Hare had deceived him, and he might go on wandering in this dark passage forever. He felt inclined to sit down and cry in despair, but there was no room to sit down, and just as he grew so tired that he thought he could go no farther, he saw in the distance a dim red light, and making one last effort he ran towards it. He found it came from a large bare vault, into which the passage led, and which was quite brilliantly lighted, though there was neither candle, lamp, nor window. On the farther side of the vault were sitting in a row what he at first believed to be six statues, but on looking a second time, to his great joy, he saw it was Handa and her five companions.

===Chinese===
齐格弗里德鼓足全部勇气，在洞沿上坐下来，轻轻溜进通道，顺着往下跑。通道里一片漆黑，窄得很，手一摆就撞到两壁，脖子一伸就碰到顶；只容他一个人直着身子走，倒是不怕迷路。他走啊走啊，左右始终没有一点光亮，没有一个开口。后来他开始喊。他喊“汉达！汉达！汉达！”那名字从四面八方回响过来，可他知道那不是汉达的声音，只是嘲弄人的回声。他怕野兔骗了他，怕自己要在这黑通道里永远走下去。他绝望得想坐下来大哭一场，可通道里连坐下的空儿都没有。就在他累得再也走不动的那一刻，他望见远处有一点暗红的光，便拼出最后的力气朝它跑去。原来光是从一个空旷的大地窖里透出来的，通道一直通进那里。地窖里亮堂堂的，却既没有蜡烛，没有灯，也没有窗户。地窖那一面，一排坐着六个他起初当作石像的身影；可他再看一眼，不由得大喜过望——那是汉达和她的五个伙伴。

===Original===
“Handa!” he cried. “Come, it is I.” But Handa never moved, but sat as if she were turned to stone, looking straight in front of her. Then Siegfrid remembered the magic shoes on her feet, and running to her pulled them off at once. Handa jumped up, and the first thing she said was, “Siegfrid, what have you done to your eye?”

===Chinese===
“汉达！”他喊道，“来呀，是我。”可汉达一动不动，像化成了石头似的坐在那儿，直勾勾望着前方。齐格弗里德这才想起她脚上的魔法鞋，跑过去一把扒了下来。汉达跳了起来，开口第一句话就是：“齐格弗里德，你的眼睛怎么了？”

===Original===
Siegfrid told her all that had happened, and how he had given his eye to the Owl, and said he did not care for the loss of his eye now that he had found her; but Handa cried, and told him that she had rather the old man had killed her than that he should have lost his eye.

===Chinese===
齐格弗里德把前后经过都告诉了她，说他怎样把眼睛给了猫头鹰，又说如今找到了她，少一只眼睛他也不放在心上；可汉达哭了，说她宁可让那老头杀了，也不愿他失去眼睛。

===Original===
Then they turned to the five other little girls, who still sat like marble figures, and Siegfrid drew the shoes from their feet, and one by one they sprang up, and thanked him for coming to save them. Siegfrid showed them the long dark passage, and then led the way up it, the children following close behind him.

===Chinese===
随后他们转向另外五个小姑娘，她们还像大理石像一样坐着。齐格弗里德把鞋从她们脚上脱下来，她们一个接一个跳起身，感谢他来救她们。齐格弗里德指给她们那条长长的黑通道，然后领头上路，孩子们紧紧跟在他身后。

===Original===
When they got to the opening in the forest, they saw that the sun was shining brightly, so that four or five hours had passed since Siegfrid had burnt the shoes, although it had seemed much less.

===Chinese===
他们走到森林的洞口，只见太阳已经明晃晃地照着——烧鞋到现在，原来已经过去四五个钟头了，虽然他们觉得没那么久。

===Original===
He helped all the children out of the hole, and then jumped out himself, but no sooner were his feet again upon the grass than the sandals fell from them and crumbled to bits, as if they too had been burnt to ashes, and at the same moment the hole in the ground closed up, and no mark was left where it had been.

===Chinese===
他把孩子们一个个托出洞口，然后自己也跳了出来。他的脚刚一沾草地，凉鞋就从脚上脱落，碎成了粉末，仿佛也被烧成了灰；与此同时，地上的洞口合拢了，连一点痕迹也没留下。

===Original===
The miller, Handa’s father, was so wearied with searching, that he was obliged to lie down to sleep that night, but at sunrise he sprang up to start again on the hunt after the lost children.

===Chinese===
汉达的父亲磨坊主连日搜寻，累得那晚不得不躺下睡了，可太阳刚一露头，他便一跃而起，又要出发寻找失踪的孩子们。

===Original===
When he was dressed he could not find his boots anywhere.

===Chinese===
他穿好衣裳，却怎么也找不着自己的靴子。

===Original===
“ ’Tis very odd,” he said, “for I know I placed them here. Wife, have you seen my boots?” But the wife said she had seen nothing of them, and, what was very strange, she could not find her own boots either.

===Chinese===
“真是怪事，”他说，“我明明把靴子搁在这儿的。孩子他妈，你见着我的靴子了吗？”妻子说没见着；更怪的是，她自己的靴子也找不着了。

===Original===
“Never mind,” quoth he, “I can walk as well without boots.”

===Chinese===
“不打紧，”他说，“没有靴子照样走路。”

===Original===
So he started with his feet bare, but when he came to the roadside where the old man was as usual putting up his stall, he paused.

===Chinese===
于是他光着一双脚上了路，走到路边，见老头照旧在支他的摊子，便停下了。

===Original===
“Neighbour,” he said, “could you let me have a pair of very cheap boots this morning? I can’t find my own anywhere.”

===Chinese===
“街坊，”他说，“今早能不能卖我一双顶便宜的靴子？我自己的那双哪儿也找不着了。”

===Original===
And the old man answered as usual﻿—

===Chinese===
老头照例唱道——

===Original===
> “Come, buy! come, buy!
 

 Shoes for all.
 

 Who’ll try? who’ll try?”

===Chinese===
> “来买呀！来买呀！
 

 鞋子人人有。
 

 谁来试？谁来试？”

===Original===
As the miller took up some of the boots to choose a pair, he looked at the old man and said, “Why, friend, what’s wrong? Are you ill? You have grown very pale and thin in the night.”

===Chinese===
磨坊主拿起几双靴子挑拣，看看老头，说：“咦，朋友，你怎么了？病了吗？一夜之间，你变得又白又瘦啊。”

===Original===
But the old man said nothing, and the miller chose a pair of boots which he thought likely to fit, and put them on. But no sooner were they on his feet than they crumbled and fell to pieces as if they had been burnt to ashes. At this the miller stared, but the little old man turned paler than before, and he began to shake with fear.

===Chinese===
老头一言不发。磨坊主挑了一双觉得合脚的靴子穿上，可靴子刚上脚，就酥了、散了，碎作一片，像烧成了灰似的。磨坊主瞪大了眼，小老头却比先前更白了，吓得直发抖。

===Original===
“Why, man,” cried the miller, “what ails the boots? They are as rotten as an old potato. Give me another pair.” So he chose another pair, and put them on, but the same thing happened again. The moment they were upon his feet they crumbled and fell away like dry ashes. At this the miller flew into a violent rage.

===Chinese===
“喂，老兄，”磨坊主喊道，“你这靴子是怎么了？烂得跟老土豆似的！再给我一双。”于是他又挑了一双穿上，可还是老样子：靴子一上脚，立刻酥裂散开，像干灰一样簌簌往下掉。磨坊主顿时勃然大怒。

===Original===
“What do you mean by selling such things?” he cried. “Give me another pair, and be sure that this time they are sound, for if they go like the last I’ll beat you black and blue. No man shall serve me such a trick three times.”

===Chinese===
“你卖这种玩意儿是什么意思？”他喊道，“再给我一双，这回可要结实的！再像刚才那样散了架，我就把你打得青一块紫一块。没人能这样连耍我三次！”

===Original===
But now the old man began to tremble in every limb, and his teeth chattered in his head with fear, whilst every moment he looked thinner and smaller, for he knew now that his wicked charms had been broken.

===Chinese===
这时老头浑身哆嗦起来，吓得牙齿在嘴里咯咯直响，人眼看着一分一分变瘦变小，因为他知道，他那些邪恶的魔咒已经全被破了。

===Original===
The miller took the third pair of boots, but they were just like the other two, and at this his rage knew no bounds. He seized the old man by the collar, to drag him into the marketplace, there to flog him soundly before all the people. But as he came near it he saw a great crowd, and there, in the centre of it, were Siegfrid and Handa and the five little girls.

===Chinese===
磨坊主拿起第三双靴子，可还是同前两双一样。这一下他的怒火再没边了，一把揪住老头的衣领，要把他拖到集市上去，当着众人的面狠狠抽他一顿。可走近集市，他看见一大群人围在那里，人群正中央，正是齐格弗里德、汉达和那五个小姑娘。

===Original===
When Handa saw her father she ran straight into his arms, and he let go the little old man, who crouched upon the ground. Siegfrid told the miller and the people all that had happened, and they all turned to look for the wicked old man who had caused all the trouble.

===Chinese===
汉达一见父亲，径直扑进他怀里；他松开了小老头，老头瘫缩在地上。齐格弗里德把事情原原本本讲给磨坊主和众人听，大家便都转过身去找那个闯下这一切大祸的恶老头。

===Original===
“What shall we do to him?” they cried.

===Chinese===
“拿他怎么办？”众人喊道。

===Original===
“How shall we punish him?”

===Chinese===
“怎么罚他才好？”

===Original===
“Let us beat him,” said one.

===Chinese===
“揍他一顿。”一个人说。

===Original===
“Let us put him in prison,” said another.

===Chinese===
“把他关起来。”另一个说。

===Original===
“Nay,” said Siegfrid, “his punishment will come of itself. See! it is coming already;” and when they looked at him, as he still crouched on the ground in front of the miller, they saw that he was already only half his former size, and that every moment he grew smaller and smaller. All the people stood staring at him and held their breath in intense astonishment, for the old man now shrank so fast that soon he would not be visible at all. Smaller and smaller he grew every moment, till at last he was nothing but a mere speck, and then he was gone altogether.

===Chinese===
“不必，”齐格弗里德说，“他的惩罚自己会来。瞧！已经来了。”众人望着还瘫在磨坊主面前的老头，只见他已缩得只剩原先一半大，而且一刻比一刻更小。众人全瞪着他，惊得大气也不敢出，因为老头缩小得那么快，眼看就要全看不见了。他一刻比一刻小，最后只剩一粒微尘，然后就彻底没有了。

===Original===
For a few moments everyone was silent; at last the miller said, “Let us all be thankful that he is gone. And now see here: Siegfrid has brought us back our girls, and has lost his eye for them; how shall we reward him?”

===Chinese===
好一会儿大家都没作声；最后磨坊主说：“他没了，咱们都该谢天谢地。再说你们瞧：齐格弗里德把咱们的姑娘们带回来了，自己却为她们丢了一只眼睛；咱们该怎么报答他呢？”

===Original===
Then all the people turned and looked at Siegfrid, who stood beside Handa.

===Chinese===
众人都转过身来，望着站在汉达身边的齐格弗里德。

===Original===
“We will give him whatever he likes best,” cried one.

===Chinese===
“他最喜欢什么，咱们就给他什么。”一个人喊道。

===Original===
“We will work for him all his life,” said another.

===Chinese===
“我们甘愿为他做一辈子工。”另一个说。

===Original===
“Nay,” said Siegfrid, “I don’t want that, but promise me that as long as my father works well for you and makes you good shoes, you will not buy them of anyone else. You brought all your troubles on yourselves by your cruelty in leaving him to starve after he had worked for you all his life.”

===Chinese===
“不必，”齐格弗里德说，“我不要这些。只要你们答应我：往后只要我父亲好好干活，给你们做好鞋，你们就不要再到别人手里买鞋。他为你们做了一辈子活计，你们却狠心让他挨饿，这些灾祸都是你们自己招来的。”

===Original===
“The boy is right,” said the miller; “we behaved very wickedly and selfishly, and we have been justly punished for our conduct.”

===Chinese===
“这孩子说得对，”磨坊主说，“我们行事又狠心又自私，受了这样的惩罚，也是罪有应得。”

===Original===
Little by little everything in the village grew better. The fever went, and the thick fogs cleared away; the cottages were rebuilt, and fresh rains fell and revived the parched grass. Still, though all soon looked bright and cheerful as ever, the people knew that it could never be the same as before the old man came to it. They knew that the charm was broken, and that theirs was no better than all the other villages in the world. But it always was a very happy home to Siegfrid, who, when he grew up, was the village shoemaker, and married Handa, and lived happily to the end of his days.

===Chinese===
村里的一切渐渐好起来。热病退了，浓雾散了；农舍重新盖起来，新雨落下，浇活了枯焦的青草。虽说不多久，村子又像从前一样明亮欢畅，可人们知道，它再也回不到老头来之前的模样了。他们知道那道护佑的魔咒已经破了，他们这村子，同世上别的村子再没有什么两样。但对齐格弗里德来说，这里永远是个极幸福的家。他长大后当了村里的鞋匠，娶了汉达，快快活活地过了一辈子。

## The Hair Tree / 发之树

===Original===
Many years ago there lived a young Queen who was said to be the most beautiful woman in the world. Her skin was white and smooth as ivory, and her eyes bright as stars. But her greatest beauty was her hair. It was neither black nor gold, but exactly halfway between, just the colour of a dead beech-leaf It was so long that it trailed behind her on the ground, and so thick that it took three maids to plait and arrange it every morning. Every day it was scented and washed by the court hairdresser, who examined it carefully to see that it was not growing thinner or falling off. The Queen’s husband, the King, was as proud of it as his wife, and gave her all sorts of lovely jewellery with which to dress it—diamond pins and golden combs—and by his special command the court gardener always kept the best flowers for the Queen to place in her hair.

===Chinese===
许多年以前，有一位年轻王后，据说她是天下最美的女子。她的肌肤白皙光滑如象牙，眼睛明亮如星辰。然而她最美的，是那一头头发。那头发既不乌黑，也不金黄，颜色恰在两者之间，正像一片枯萎的山毛榉叶。它长得拖曳到身后地上，浓密得每天早晨要三个侍女才编得过来、理得过来。宫廷理发师天天给它熏香、洗濯，还细细查验，唯恐它日渐稀疏或脱落。王后的丈夫国王同她一样为这头发自豪，赐给她各式各样美丽的首饰来装扮它——钻石发针、金梳——还特谕宫廷花匠，总是把最好的花留给王后簪发。

===Original===
And not only the King and Queen, but all the courtiers and court ladies—indeed, everyone in the country—praised and admired this beautiful hair; and although some of the court ladies were rather jealous of it, yet all agreed that it would be a real national misfortune if any harm came to it.

===Chinese===
不但国王和王后，满朝的廷臣命妇——实在说，全国上下——都称赞、艳羡这头美发；虽有几位宫廷命妇对它颇为嫉妒，可众口一词：这头发若有闪失，实在是举国的大不幸。

===Original===
One morning the Queen was sitting at her window at work, when a big bird flew past. It was much like an eagle, with a hooked beak and ugly fierce eyes. It hovered around the window for some time, and at last settled on a tree outside and watched the Queen, who did not look up till she was surprised by hearing the bird say in a croaking voice—

===Chinese===
一天早晨，王后正坐在窗前做活计，一只大鸟飞掠而过。那鸟样子很有些像鹰，弯弯的喙，一双凶恶的丑眼睛。它在窗边盘桓了好一阵，最后落在外头一棵树上，盯着王后瞧。王后起初没有抬头，直到那鸟沙哑着嗓子开了口，才把她吓了一跳——

===Original===
“Good day, Queen; you’ve plenty of hair.”

===Chinese===
“日安，王后；您的头发真多啊。”

===Original===
The Queen laughed, well pleased that even a common eagle should notice her beautiful tresses.

===Chinese===
王后笑了，心想连一只寻常的鹰也留意到她的美发，好不得意。

===Original===
“Yes,” she said, shaking it around her. “I’ve more than any other lady in the land.”

===Chinese===
“不错，”她说着，把头发往四下一抖。“全国没有哪位夫人比我的多。”

===Original===
“Then you’ve enough to give me a little,” said the bird. “I can’t find anything soft to line my nest with, and some of your hair would do nicely.”

===Chinese===
“那您尽可以分我一点儿，”鸟说，“我找不到柔软的东西垫窝，您的头发正合适。”

===Original===
“My hair!” cried the Queen, staring at the bird in astonishment; “my beautiful hair to line your common nest with! You must be mad. Do you know that I’m the Queen, and that I value my hair more than anything on earth?”

===Chinese===
“我的头发！”王后喊起来，惊愕地瞪着那鸟；“拿我美丽的头发去垫你那寒酸的窝！你准是疯了。你可知道我是王后，我的头发比世上什么都金贵？”

===Original===
“Nevertheless, it would do very nicely for my nest, and I advise you to give me some of it,” croaked the eagle.

===Chinese===
“话虽如此，它垫我的窝实在再好不过，我劝你还是给我一些罢。”鹰哑着嗓子说。

===Original===
“Indeed, I shall do nothing of the kind,” said the Queen, eagerly. “I never heard such impertinence in my life. Fly away immediately, or I will send out some of the soldiers to shoot you.”

===Chinese===
“哼，我偏不。”王后急赤白脸地说。“我平生没听见过这样无礼的话。快飞走，不然我就叫士兵出来开枪打你。”

===Original===
“They couldn’t do it,” said the bird, with a low laugh; “and if you tell them to try, you will be sorry for it afterwards. Now, I shall only give you one more chance. Queen, will you give me some of your hair for my nest?”

===Chinese===
“他们打不着，”鸟低声一笑，“你若叫他们来试，日后有得后悔。如今我只再给你一次机会。王后，你肯不肯给我一些头发垫窝？”

===Original===
“No, I shall not,” answered the Queen, half crying with anger; “it is very presumptuous of you to ask for such a thing.”

===Chinese===
“不肯，就是不肯。”王后答道，气得半带哭腔。“你讨这样的东西，好大的胆子。”

===Original===
The bird said no more, but rising into the air began to fly slowly round and round the tree on which he had been perched, and as he flew he sang these words in a low voice—

===Chinese===
鸟不再言语，腾身空中，绕着方才栖过的那棵树缓缓盘旋，一面飞，一面低声唱道——

===Original===
> “As the wind blows this tree’s twigs bare,
 

 So shall the proud Queen lose her hair;
 

 The leaves shall come back with the first spring rain,
 

 But when shall the Queen find her hair again?”

===Chinese===
> “风吹得这树的细枝精光，
 

 高傲的王后头发也要落光；
 

 初春一降雨，叶子还会回来，
 

 王后的头发，何时才能回来？”

===Original===
When he had done, the eagle gave a shrill cry and disappeared, leaving the Queen in astonishment at his conduct.

===Chinese===
唱罢，鹰长唳一声，霎时不见了，撇下王后，为它这番行径惊愕不已。

===Original===
It was autumn, and she heard the wind whistling in the branches, and presently a number of leaves dropped from the cherry-tree, and fluttered to the ground, and, at the same moment, a handful of her curls fell out into her lap.

===Chinese===
时值秋天，她听得风在枝头呼啸。不多时，樱桃树上飘下片片叶子，打着旋儿落了地；就在同一刻，她鬓边一把卷发也脱落下来，散在她的裙兜里。

===Original===
The Queen started up in alarm, and ran with tears in her eyes to the King to tell him what had happened. The King laughed at her fears, assuring her the bird could do her no harm, and that it was only by chance that her hair fell out just then.

===Chinese===
王后惊慌地跳起来，含着眼泪跑去把经过告诉国王。国王笑她多虑，叫她放心，说那鸟害不了她，头发恰好那时脱落，不过碰巧罢了。

===Original===
Yet the Queen could not feel comfortable, and that night, when her ladies were brushing her hair, a quantity came out on the brushes and two or three locks fell to the ground. Next morning when she awoke she found some long soft brown tresses lying loose on her pillow, and when she got out of bed, a whole shower fell from her head to the floor.

===Chinese===
