可是王后心里总不踏实。当夜侍女们替她梳头，梳子上挂下来好些头发，还有两三绺落在地上。第二天早晨醒来，她见枕上散着几绺又长又软的褐色头发；待她下得床来，满头头发簌簌而落，像下了一阵雨。

===Original===
The Queen wrung her hands in despair, and at once sent for the court hairdresser, who came, bringing with him a number of bottles, all containing lotions to make the hair grow; but all the lotions in the world were of no use. There was no denying the fact that the Queen’s hair was falling out in a most alarming manner. There was scarcely enough for her ladies to plait, and a bald spot was beginning to show on the top of her head. She dared not look at the cherry-tree to see if its branches were beginning to look bare also, but at every gust she heard the leaves falling, and trembled as she thought how soon they would all be gone. At last one morning, after the wind had been very fierce and strong, she ran to the window, and, looking out, saw that the cherry-tree’s branches were quite bare—not a leaf to be seen anywhere. Then she turned to her looking-glass, and, dreadful to behold, she was quite bald also. Her last lock of hair had fallen off and left her head as smooth and white as an egg. At this fearful sight the Queen screamed and fainted, and when her ladies came to help her they were all so shocked that they could scarcely speak. As for the King, his grief was so great that he sobbed aloud.

===Chinese===
王后绝望地绞着双手，立刻宣宫廷理发师进见。理发师来了，带来一大堆瓶子，瓶瓶都装着生发药水；可天下的药水全不管用。无可讳言：王后的头发正以骇人的速度脱落。剩下的几乎不够侍女们编辫子了，头顶上也渐渐显出一块秃斑。她不敢去看那棵樱桃树，不敢看它的枝条是否也开始光秃；可每一阵风起，她都听得见叶子簌簌落下，一想它们转眼就要落尽，便浑身发抖。终于有一天早晨，狂风大作过后，她奔到窗前朝外一望——樱桃树的枝条已经精光，一片叶子也寻不见了。她转过身来照镜，天哪，可怕的是：她自己也全秃了。最后一绺头发已经脱落，她的头光光白白，滑得像一枚鸡蛋。见这可怕的景象，王后尖叫一声，晕了过去；侍女们赶来救她，个个惊得说不出话来。至于国王，他悲痛得放声哭了。

===Original===
The Queen was laid on her bed, and the court physicians and hairdressers held a great consultation as to what was to be done to make her hair grow, and it was agreed that her head should go through a regular course of treatment. In the meantime she said she would keep her room, and it was given out that she was very ill, in order that the common people might not know what a dreadful thing had happened. But all the doctors and hairdressers could do nothing. Not a single hair reappeared on the Queen’s bare white head, and it was impossible to conceal the fact any longer. There was great sorrow all over the country when it became known, and a general mourning was proclaimed till the Queen’s hair should have grown again. In the meantime she wore a little lace cap trimmed with jewels. It was a very pretty little cap, and very becoming—so, at least, all the courtiers said. But it was not so beautiful as her own hair, and the Queen felt this so much that the first time she appeared in public in her cap she could scarcely help crying.

===Chinese===
王后被安放到床上，御医和理发师们会诊良久，商议如何叫头发重新长出，议定要给她的头按部就班地调治一番。这期间她说要闭门静养，宫里便放出话来，只说王后病得厉害，免得百姓知道出了这等可怕的事。可是医生理发师们百般施为，全然无用。王后那光白的头上，连一根头发也没再冒出来，事实再也瞒不住了。消息传开，举国哀伤，全国挂孝志哀，直到王后的头发重新长出为止。这期间她戴一顶镶宝石的花边小帽。那是顶挺好看的小帽，戴着也颇相宜——至少廷臣们个个这么说。可它终究不及她自己的头发美，王后深以为苦，头一回戴着小帽露面时，几乎忍不住要哭出来。

===Original===
So things went on for a little time, and the doctors and hairdressers were all racking their brains to find something to make hair grow. But the Queen fretted so much that she lost her appetite and really fell ill, and was obliged to keep to her bed. The first night after her illness she dreamt a dream, which she thought about all day long. She dreamt that she was sitting in the palace garden, when there came up a little man, who was the strangest object she had ever seen. He was no bigger than a big spider, and all dressed in green. When he saw her he began to dance and sing this rhyme:—

===Chinese===
这样过了些时日，医生理发师们个个绞尽脑汁，要寻出生发的东西来。可是王后忧思过甚，茶饭无心，竟真的病倒了，只得卧床。病后头一夜，她做了一个梦，白天整整想了一天。她梦见自己坐在御花园里，走来一个小人儿，那是她平生所见最古怪的东西。他不过一只大蜘蛛那么大，周身穿着绿衣裳。他一见她，便跳起舞来，唱起这支歌谣：——

===Original===
> “When the grass is thin, you must mow it, mow it, mow it,
 

 But when the ground is bare, you must sow it, sow it, sow it.”

===Chinese===
> “草儿稀了，就得割它、割它、割它，
 

 地皮光了呢，就得种它、种它、种它。”

===Original===
And as he sang these words he took out a handful of tiny seeds from his pocket, and began to scatter them about. Directly there sprang up a little plant, which grew and grew and grew until it became a big tree, and then it put forth buds, and the buds burst, and there came out not leaves, but tiny locks of hair, which grew and grew until they touched the ground, and covered the tree all over.

===Chinese===
他一面唱，一面从口袋里掏出一把细小的种子，四下撒开。登时冒出一株小苗，长啊，长啊，长啊，长成了一棵大树；接着树上生出蓓蕾，蓓蕾绽开，出来的不是叶子，而是一绺绺细小的头发，又长啊长啊，一直垂到地上，把整棵树罩得严严实实。

===Original===
When the Queen awoke she could think of nothing but the strange Hair Tree; and that night when she went to sleep she dreamt exactly the same thing. On the third night she had the same dream again, and when she awoke she called to her husband, and told him of what she had dreamt three nights running.

===Chinese===
王后一觉醒来，满脑子只有那棵奇异的发之树（Hair Tree）；当夜睡去，梦见的分毫不差又是它。第三夜还是同一个梦。她醒来便唤丈夫，把接连三夜梦见的情景讲给他听。

===Original===
“And now, my dear husband,” she continued, “I am sure that the only thing which can ever make my hair grow is some seeds from the Hair Tree. I entreat you to spare no effort to discover it, and to offer a reward to anyone who will bring you news of it. For I feel”—here the Queen’s voice grew very husky—“that if I do not find it I shall never recover my hair, and if not, I shall die, for I cannot live without it.” So saying she fell back on the pillow and closed her eyes.

===Chinese===
“如今，亲爱的夫君，”她接着说，“我确信，唯一能使我头发重新长出的，就是发之树上的种子。我求你竭尽全力去寻它，悬赏购求它的消息，凡带来音讯的人都给重赏。因为我觉着——”说到这里，王后的声音哽住了——“若寻不着它，我的头发就永远好不了；头发好不了，我也活不成了，没有它我万难苟活。”说罢，她跌回枕上，闭上了眼睛。

===Original===
The King at once called a great meeting of his lords together, and told them the Queen’s dream, and a herald was sent round to proclaim that one hundred thousand pounds would be given to anyone who could bring some seeds from the Hair Tree, or even tell where it was to be found. And great placards were pasted up all over the kingdom, offering the reward. So everyone began to talk of this strange Hair Tree, which no one had ever heard of before, and to wonder where it could be—for all would have liked to have earned the reward—but all agreed that the Hair Tree was nowhere in that country.

===Chinese===
国王立刻召集众勋贵大开朝议，把王后的梦讲了一遍，又派出传令官四处宣谕：凡能取来发之树种子、哪怕只是说出它下落的人，赏十万英镑。王国各处张贴了悬赏的大幅告示。于是人人都在谈论这棵奇异的发之树——先前谁也不曾听说过——都在猜想它究竟在哪里，因为谁都盼着赚那笔赏金；只是众口一词：发之树断不会在本国之内。

===Original===
Now it chanced that a poor sailor named Rupert saw one of the placards, and stopped to read it. He was a strong young man, but he had neither father nor mother, nor sisters nor brothers, and he felt so lonely that he did not care what became of him.

===Chinese===
且说有个穷水手，名叫鲁珀特（Rupert），碰巧看见一张告示，便站住读了起来。他是个结实的小伙子，却无父无母，无姐无妹，孤零零一个人，觉着自己落到什么地步都无所谓。

===Original===
So when he saw the placard and the large reward that was offered to anyone who could bring news of the Hair Tree, he began to wonder where it was, and if he could not find it.

===Chinese===
他见了告示，见到那笔给寻得发之树音讯之人的巨额赏金，便琢磨起它到底在哪里，自己能不能把它找到。

===Original===
“One hundred thousand pounds is a lot of money,” he said to himself. “There is no reason why I should not find it as well as another man. I have a good mind to go and look for it, though it seems rather a wild-goose chase.”

===Chinese===
“十万英镑可不是小数目，”他自言自语，“旁人找得，凭什么我找不得？我倒真想动身去找找，虽说这事瞧着多半像大海捞针。”

===Original===
So he packed up his things, and took a little boat, and sailed away towards the north; for it was there, he knew, was a strange country where animals speak like men and women, and plants have hands and eyes; and he thought that there he might find the wonderful Hair Tree.

===Chinese===
于是他收拾好行李，驾了一条小船，朝北边驶去；因为他知道，北方有一片奇异的国度，那里的走兽像男女一样开口说话，草木生着手和眼睛；他想，兴许在那儿能寻见那奇妙的发之树。

===Original===
In the meantime half the young men in the kingdom had started off to search, hoping to win the reward. Some went east, some went west, some north, some south; and they sought in every imaginable place and country. Some gave it up very soon, and returned home, saying that there was no such tree. Others went on and on, asking everyone they met if they had heard of or seen it, and finding no trace of it, yet, being determined not to give it up and return home empty-handed, went still farther and farther. And so months passed away, and the Queen’s head still remained bald.

===Chinese===
这时候，王国里半数年轻人已经动身寻树去了，都巴望赢得那笔赏金。有的往东，有的往西，有的往北，有的往南；凡是想得到的地方和国家，他们统统寻了个遍。有的不久便灰了心，回家说根本没这么棵树。有的则一程又一程地走，逢人便打听可曾听说过、可曾见过它，虽不见半点踪迹，却又打定主意不肯罢休、不肯空手回家，于是越走越远。就这样几个月过去，王后的头依旧光着。

===Original===
In order that her Majesty might not be annoyed by the sight of other women’s hair when her own was gone, the King ordered that they should all wear little caps which quite covered the head and came down on the forehead; and because no one liked these caps, and all thought them very unbecoming, the court ladies were almost as anxious as the Queen that the tree should be found, and her hair grow again.

===Chinese===
为了不让王后陛下瞧见别人的头发烦心，国王下旨：全国妇女一律戴上小帽，把头包严，直压到额前。这些帽子谁也不喜欢，人人都说不像样子，于是宫廷命妇们几乎同王后一样急切，只盼那棵树快快寻到，王后的头发快快长回。

===Original===
Rupert sailed and sailed to the north till he began to think he must be coming to the magic country of which he had heard. For a long time he went on without seeing land anywhere, but at last he came in sight of a little island standing quite alone in the middle of the water. On it there grew neither grass nor flowers of any sort, but three solitary trees. One was much like a common nut-tree, only all the nuts were of a bright red colour; the boughs of the second were laden with precious stones of all kinds, diamonds and rubies, emeralds and pearls; but the third tree was the strangest of all, for its boughs were quite bare, and looked as if they were made of polished brass, and on the centre at the top grew an enormous pod, which pointed straight at the sky. It looked like a great brass drum.

===Chinese===
鲁珀特朝北驶啊驶啊，驶到后来，他想自己准是快到传闻中的那个魔法国度了。好长时间里他四望不见陆地，末了终于望见汪洋正中孤零零一座小岛。岛上寸草不生，一朵花也没有，只立着三棵孤零零的树。一棵很有些像寻常的坚果树，只是满树的坚果都是鲜红色；第二棵的枝头缀满各色宝石——钻石、红宝石、祖母绿、珍珠；第三棵却顶顶古怪：枝条精光，看去像擦得锃亮的黄铜，树顶正中结着一个巨大的豆荚，直指青天，模样像一面大黄铜鼓。

===Original===
Rupert pulled up his boat, and, springing on to the island, filled his pockets full of precious stones and common nuts, and then stood still looking at the enormous pod and wondering what it could contain. While he watched it there was a noise like a clap of thunder, and the pod burst asunder, and twelve round shining golden nuts fell to the ground. At the same time the tree withered as if it had been struck by lightning, and the brass branches all fell away.

===Chinese===
鲁珀特把船拢上岸，跳上小岛，往口袋里塞满了宝石和红坚果，然后站定下来，望着那只巨大的豆荚出神，猜它里头究竟装着什么。正看着，忽听一声巨响如雷，豆荚轰然裂开，十二颗圆滚滚、亮闪闪的金坚果落了一地。与此同时，那树立刻枯萎，像遭了雷殛，铜枝纷纷坠落。

===Original===
Rupert was so frightened by the noise the pod made in bursting that he had buried his face in the ground, but when he found all was quiet again, he slowly raised himself, and picked up one of the gold nuts to examine it. As they were pretty and bright, he thought he would take them all for curiosities, and he placed them on the seat of his little boat. But as he rowed away from the island he began to feel sure he was coming to the enchanted land. The fishes no longer started away from the side of the boat, but swam after it in a long trail, and when he baited his line and threw it overboard not one would bite, but swam up quite close to the hook and then turned away, giving little low, scoffing laughs. Rupert was sorely puzzled, and sat looking at the water in wonder, when he heard a whirring of wings overhead, and a great yellow bird flew round the boat, and at last perched on the prow and peered curiously into his face.

===Chinese===
豆荚爆裂的声响吓得鲁珀特把脸埋进地里；待到四下重新静了，他才慢慢抬起身来，拾起一颗金坚果细看。这些坚果又漂亮又光亮，他想不如统统带走当个稀罕物，便把它们摆在小船的座板上。可是驶离小岛不久，他渐渐觉得，自己准是到了那片魔法之地了。鱼儿不再从船边惊散，反而排成长长一串跟在船后；他上了鱼饵，把钓线抛进水里，竟没有一条肯咬钩的，都游到钩子跟前，偏偏又扭头游开，发出低低的讥笑。鲁珀特纳闷极了，坐在那里望着水面发怔。忽然头顶响起一阵扑翅声，一只大黄鸟绕着船飞，末了落在船头，好奇地端详他的脸。

===Original===
After a few minutes it said, “Are those nuts you have in your pocket?”

===Chinese===
过了一会儿它开口了：“你口袋里装的是坚果么？”

===Original===
Rupert said “Yes,” and drawing out a handful of the red nuts, offered them to the bird.

===Chinese===
鲁珀特说“是”，掏出一把红坚果，递给那鸟。

===Original===
“Crack some and give me the kernels,” said the bird. Rupert obeyed, afraid to refuse, as he remembered what a dreadful thing had happened to the poor Queen for not being polite to a bird. The bird went on eating the nuts, and then said—

===Chinese===
“敲开几颗，把仁儿给我。”鸟说。鲁珀特照办了，不敢回绝——他记得可怜的王后只因对一只鸟失礼，就遭了多大的殃。鸟吃个不停，随后说道——

===Original===
“Where are you going?”

===Chinese===
“你要上哪儿去？”

===Original===
Then the sailor told how the Queen had been rude to a bird, who had in return destroyed her hair.

===Chinese===
于是水手讲起来：王后如何怠慢了一只鸟，那鸟又如何害得她头发掉光。

===Original===
“I know; that was me,” said the bird, with a chuckle. “Give me some more nuts.”

===Chinese===
“我知道；那正是我干的好事。”鸟咯咯一笑。“再给我点儿坚果。”

===Original===
Rupert again handed the bird the nuts, and then went on relating how the Queen had had a strange dream of a wonderful Hair Tree, and how the King had offered a reward to anyone who could find it; so he was going to look for it.

===Chinese===
鲁珀特又把坚果递给它，接着讲下去：王后如何梦见一棵奇妙的发之树，国王如何悬赏寻它的人；所以他这才动身去找。

===Original===
“You will search a long time for the Hair Tree, I expect,” said the bird, still munching. “If the Queen waits for her hair till it is found, she will have to do without it nearly all her life. I suppose she’ll wish now that she had been civil.” But as he finished speaking, the bird’s glance fell on the golden nuts lying on the seat, and with a shrill cry he flew at them; but Rupert had seized them first and held them firmly out of his reach.

===Chinese===
“依我看，你这发之树可得找上好一阵子呢。”鸟嘴里嚼着说。“王后若要等找到树才长头发，只怕大半辈子都得光着脑袋了。我猜她如今总该盼着自己当初客气点儿了。”话音未落，鸟的目光落在座板上那几颗金坚果上，尖啸一声便扑了过去；可是鲁珀特抢先一把攥住，紧紧拿到它够不着的地方。

===Original===
“What are these?” gasped the bird. “Where did you get them? Tell me at once.”

===Chinese===
“这是什么？”鸟喘着气问。“你从哪儿弄来的？快告诉我。”

===Original===
“They came,” said Rupert, still holding the bag tightly, “from the single pod of a tree that has no leaves, and whose branches look like brass, and that grows on a little island not far from here.” On hearing this the bird gave a low cry, and crouched down on one side of the boat, where it sat eyeing Rupert and the nuts greedily, and shaking with rage. “Then they are the nuts of the zirbal-tree,” he said at last; “and the pod has broken when I was not there. For two thousand years have I waited for that pod to break, and now it will be two thousand years before it is ripe again, and it is the only zirbal-tree living, and there is nothing on earth like its nuts.”

===Chinese===
“它们么，”鲁珀特说，手仍攥得紧紧的，“来自一棵不长叶子的树，那树枝条像黄铜，顶上只结了一只豆荚，就长在离这儿不远的一座小岛上。”鸟一听，低低叫了一声，缩到船舷一边蹲下，眼巴巴盯着鲁珀特和那几颗坚果，气得浑身发抖。“这么说，这些是齐尔巴尔树（zirbal-tree）的坚果了，”它终于开口，“豆荚偏偏在我离开时裂开。两千年了，我等那豆荚裂开等了整整两千年，如今它要再过两千年才能结熟；那是世上仅存的一棵齐尔巴尔树，它的坚果，天下再没有第二样。”

===Original===
When Rupert saw the bird crouched in one corner of the seat, with its head lowered and its feathers ruffled, and remembered how much mischief it had done, he felt strongly inclined to seize it and wring its neck, but he restrained himself, and said—

===Chinese===
鲁珀特见那鸟缩在座板一角，耷拉着脑袋，羽毛蓬乱，又想起它干的那些坏事，恨不得一把掐住它的脖子拧了，可还是忍住了，说道——

===Original===
“You had better sit up. I’ve got the nuts, and I mean to keep them; but perhaps, if you’ll answer me one or two questions, I’ll give you one.”

===Chinese===
“你最好坐直了。坚果在我手里，我是不会撒手的；不过，你要是肯回答我一两个问题，我可以给你一颗。”

===Original===
On this the bird drew itself up, and, arranging its ruffled plumage, sat watching Rupert suspiciously.

===Chinese===
听了这话，鸟挺起身来，理了理乱羽，蹲在那里满腹狐疑地盯着鲁珀特。

===Original===
“You say you’re the bird who charmed away the Queen’s hair. Well, if you’ll tell me how she can grow it again I’ll give you a nut.”

===Chinese===
“你说你就是施法害走王后头发的那只鸟。好，你若肯告诉我她怎样才能重新长出头发，我就给你一颗坚果。”

===Original===
“What!” cried the bird, flapping his wings angrily, “tell you how the Queen can recover her hair? Never—I’d sooner never taste a nut again.”

===Chinese===
“什么！”鸟怒气冲冲地拍着翅膀喊道，“告诉你王后怎么把头发找回来？休想——我宁可从此再不尝一颗坚果！”

===Original===
“Very well,” said Rupert, picking up a golden nut, and holding it temptingly towards the bird. “Only tell me which way I ought to steer to find the country where the Hair Tree grows, and I will give you this.”

===Chinese===
“很好，”鲁珀特说着，拾起一颗金坚果，在鸟跟前晃来晃去地逗它。“那你只消告诉我，该朝哪个方向驶，才能找到长发之树的地方，我就把这颗给你。”

===Original===
The bird sat silent, with his head on one side, watching Rupert for some time, and then with a sudden cry rose into the air and was out of sight almost before he knew it had moved.

===Chinese===
鸟歪着脑袋坐在那里，一声不响，盯着鲁珀特看了半晌，忽然尖叫一声腾空而去，几乎不等他明白过来，就没了影儿。

===Original===
At first he felt angry at this, but as it was gone, it was no use thinking about it, and, anyhow, he had learnt that the golden nuts were very valuable, and he thought if it wanted them so much it might perhaps return for them.

===Chinese===
起初他很生气，可鸟已经飞了，气也无用；况且不管怎样，他已知道这些金坚果十分贵重，心想它既然这么眼馋，说不定还会回来讨的。

===Original===
He was right. All day and night he drifted about without coming in sight of land, but next morning at sunrise he saw, in the far distance, a dim line, and at the same moment he heard a whirring of wings overhead, and the Eagle flew down and perched on the edge of the boat as before.

===Chinese===
他料得不错。他四处漂流了一天一夜，始终望不见陆地；第二天早晨日出时分，他远远望见一线模糊的影子，就在同一刻，头顶响起扑翅之声，那只鹰飞落下来，同先前一样停在船舷上。

===Original===
Rupert remained quiet, without seeming to notice it, until at last it broke silence with—

===Chinese===
鲁珀特不动声色，装作没有留意它，末了还是它先开了口——

===Original===
“Are you in the same mind as last night? I’ll tell you the way to the Hair Tree country for a zirbal nut.”

===Chinese===
“你还抱着昨夜的主意么？告诉我去发之树地方的路，换一颗齐尔巴尔坚果。”

===Original===
The sailor took one out of his pocket, and said—

===Chinese===
水手从口袋里摸出一颗，说——

===Original===
“Tell me first, then, and you shall have the nut afterwards.”

===Chinese===
“那你先说，说完坚果归你。”

===Original===
“That is the country where the Hair Tree grows, in front of you,” said the bird. “Many go to it, but very few return.”

===Chinese===
“长发之树的地方就在你眼前，”鸟说，“去的人多，回来的可少。”

===Original===
“Why is that?” asked Rupert, as he gave the bird the nut.

===Chinese===
“这是为什么？”鲁珀特一边问，一边把坚果递给它。

===Original===
“Why?” repeated the bird, seizing it with his bill. “You had better go there and find out. You know now where it is.”

===Chinese===
“为什么？”鸟用喙叼住坚果，重复了一遍。“你最好自己去弄明白。如今你知道它在哪儿了。”

===Original===
Rupert pondered a little; then he said, “If you will tell me how to find my way safely to the Hair Tree and back, when I pass here again I will give you six whole nuts. And if you refuse I shall take my gun and shoot you.”

===Chinese===
鲁珀特沉吟片刻，说道：“你若肯告诉我怎样平安到得发之树、再平安回来，等我再经过这里，就给你整整六颗坚果。你要是不肯，我就端起枪来打你。”

===Original===
The bird gave a scornful laugh. “You couldn’t shoot me,” he said; “you can try if you like. Why, if it were not for the magic fruit in your pocket, you wouldn’t be here now. It is only that which has protected you from my spells. Well, perhaps I will do what you want, and tell you how to find your way to the Hair Tree; only, you must promise faithfully to give me the nuts when you return.”

===Chinese===
鸟轻蔑地一笑。“你打不着我的，”它说，“你尽管试。嘿，要不是你口袋里那魔法果子，你这会儿早没命了。全靠它护着你，才破了我的法术。好吧，你想要的，我兴许肯照办，告诉你怎么找到发之树；只是你得老老实实答应，回来时把坚果给我。”

===Original===
The sailor promised again, and the bird continued—

===Chinese===
水手又应承了一遍，鸟接着说——

===Original===
“When you reach land you can go on shore at any place—it doesn’t matter where—and walk straight on. You will find that all the animals can speak; but I advise you not to speak to any of them. And, whatever you do, beware of the plants and flowers; for they all have hands and arms, and will try to seize you, and if once they get you nothing can save you—no, not even your magic nuts. You must go on till you come to a high wall, in which is a heavy iron door, over which is written—

===Chinese===
“你到了岸，随便什么地方都好上岸——在哪儿上都无所谓——只管一直往前走。你会发现那里的走兽个个会说话；可我劝你谁也别搭理。还有，千万当心那些花草树木；它们全都生着手和臂膀，会来捉你，一旦叫它们拿住，什么都救不了你——对，连你的魔法坚果也不中用。你要一直走到一堵高墙跟前，墙上有一扇厚重的铁门，门上写着——

===Original===
> ‘Only he who knows can enter here,
 

 Yet only he can know, who enters without fear.’

===Chinese===
> “唯有知晓之人，方能进入此门；
 

 唯有无惧而入之人，方能知晓。”

===Original===
And you must stand before it, and say—

===Chinese===
你必须站在门前，念道——

===Original===
> ‘I know, I know;
 

 Inside the wind does blow,
 

 Inside the waters flow;
 

 This makes the Hair Tree grow—
 

 I know, I know.’

===Chinese===
> “我知道，我知道；
 

 里面有风吹不休，
 

 里面有水长长流，
 

 风吹水淌，发之树才长得秀——
 

 我知道，我知道。”

===Original===
Then the doors will fly open, and you can pass in. Within grows the Hair Tree. What else you will find there I shall not say. Now, I have kept my part of the agreement; see that you keep yours when you return.”

===Chinese===
“门便会豁然大开，你就可以进去。发之树就长在里面。至于你还会在那里遇见什么，我不说。好了，我已践了我的约；回来时你的那份，可要守好。”

===Original===
So saying, the bird flapped his wings once and disappeared.

===Chinese===
说罢，鸟振了振翅，不见了。

===Original===
Rupert looked about, and finding himself very near land, rowed to shore, and stepped from his boat. It seemed to be a pretty sort of country, and at first he did not notice anything unusual. He walked straight on till he came to a row of splendid sunflowers, and stopped in front of them to admire them, when all at once a pair of beautiful white hands and arms were thrust out from the nearest stalk, and grasping him by the shoulders began to pull him, with a strength he could not resist. At once all the other stalks had put out their hands, and were reaching towards him. And from above the large yellow flowers there appeared heads—women’s heads—with beautiful dreamy faces and bright golden hair. In another moment Rupert felt the arms would have crushed him to death, for they were fast tightening their hold, and felt like iron hands.

===Chinese===
鲁珀特四下一望，见离陆地已经很近，便划到岸边，跨下船来。这地方瞧着倒挺秀美，起初他并未察觉什么异样。他径直往前走，走到一排灿然的向日葵跟前，停下脚步观赏；冷不防，最近的一根花茎里伸出一双雪白美丽的手和臂膀，抓住他的双肩便往里拽，那力气大得他挣不脱。霎时间，其余的花茎也都伸出手来，朝他够过来。硕大的黄花上方，探出一张张脑袋——女人的脑袋——脸庞美得恍如梦境，一头头金发灿亮。转眼间鲁珀特就觉得那双臂膀要把他勒死了：它们越箍越紧，硬得像铁钳。

===Original===
Suddenly he remembered the jewels in his pockets, and drawing out a brilliant emerald, flashed it before the face of the sunflower in whose arms he was. “Leave me alone,” he cried, “and you shall have this.”

===Chinese===
他猛然想起口袋里的宝石，掏出一颗莹润的祖母绿，在抱住他的那株向日葵脸前晃了晃。“放开我，”他喊道，“这个就归你。”

===Original===
But still the arms bound him with their strong grasp, and gave no sign of loosening their hold. Then Rupert took the jewel and, holding it above his head, said, “If you don’t let me go at once, I will throw it beyond your reach, so that you will never get it.”

===Chinese===
可那臂膀依旧死死箍着他，一点松手的迹象也没有。鲁珀特便把宝石高高举过头顶，说：“你若不立刻放开我，我就把它扔到你够不着的地方，叫你永远得不到。”

===Original===
For a moment the arms wavered, then they slowly gave way, and Rupert burst from them trembling at the danger from which he had escaped. With haste, and still keeping at arms’ length, he dropped the jewel into the cruel beautiful white hand that was stretched out to receive it. Then he ran as fast as he could from the row of sunflowers and sat down, still panting and trembling, on a large bare stone.

===Chinese===
那双臂膀迟疑了一下，才慢慢松开。鲁珀特挣脱出来，想到方才险些丧命，兀自发抖。他急忙把宝石丢进那只伸出来接的雪白美丽而狠毒的手里，仍旧隔着一条手臂的距离不敢靠近。随后他撒腿逃离那排向日葵，跑到一块光秃秃的大石头上坐下，兀自气喘吁吁、浑身发抖。

===Original===
After he had a little recovered himself, he looked about him, and perceived that not only the sunflowers but all the trees had hands and arms, which they were always stretching to try and catch something, and on looking on the ground he saw that even the blades of grass were armed with tiny hands, which they kept stretching into the air.

===Chinese===
他稍稍定下神来，环顾四周，这才看出不但向日葵，连所有的树木都长着手和臂膀，总在四下伸探，想抓住点什么；再看地上，连一根根草叶也都武装着小小的手，不住向空中伸张。

===Original===
For amusement he buried his hand in the grass, and in an instant it was clutched by a hundred tiny hands, that pulled it hard. He could easily draw it away, for it was no more than if a number of spiders had seized his hand. He wondered much at this country, where all the plants seemed to be alive and he had seen no trace of man or beast. While he was thinking, a shadow fell across his path, and looking up he saw a large handsome striped tigress standing in front of him looking into his face. He at once put his hand to his gun, and would have shot it if it had not said in a quiet voice—

===Chinese===
他一时好玩，把手插进草里，霎时被上百只小手紧紧抓住，用力地拽。他不难把手抽回，那感觉不过是像一群蜘蛛扒住了他的手。他对这个国度惊叹不已：草木仿佛都有生命，而人迹兽踪却半点不见。正想着，一片影子落在他跟前的路上。他抬头一看，一头高大漂亮的斑纹母虎站在面前，端详着他的脸。他立刻伸手去摸枪，若不是母虎平静地开了口，他早已开枪了——

===Original===
“Why are you going to shoot me? I shall do you no harm. How did you come here? You are the first man who has been on this island since I came.”

===Chinese===
“你为什么开枪打我？我不会伤害你。你是怎么到这儿来的？自我来到这岛上，你是头一个人。”

===Original===
Rupert stared in astonishment at hearing a tigress speak so plainly, and said—

===Chinese===
听见母虎口齿这般清楚，鲁珀特惊得直瞪眼，说——

===Original===
“I came in a little boat. My name is Rupert, and I am come to look for the Hair Tree. Who are you? I never heard a tigress speak before.”

===Chinese===
“我坐小船来的。我叫鲁珀特，是来找发之树的。你是谁？我从来没听过母虎说人话。”

===Original===
“I am not really a tigress; I am a woman who was turned into a tigress by a wicked fairy. Tell me what you want with the Hair Tree, and how you managed to get here alive, and I will tell you my story.”

===Chinese===
“我其实并不是母虎；我是个女人，被一个邪恶的仙女变成了母虎。告诉我你要发之树做什么，你又是怎么活着到这儿来的，我就把我的身世讲给你听。”

===Original===
So Rupert told the tigress all that had happened to him; how he had set out to find the Hair Tree, and how he had picked up the nuts of the zirbal-tree, and his promise to the yellow bird, and how he had only just escaped from the sunflowers.

===Chinese===
于是鲁珀特把自己的遭遇一五一十讲给母虎听：他怎样动身寻找发之树，怎样拾了齐尔巴尔树的坚果，怎样应承了那只黄鸟，又怎样险险逃脱向日葵的手。

===Original===
She listened quietly; then she said, “You owe your life to the nuts of the zirbal-tree. They are enchanted, and that is why the yellow eagle could not kill you at once, as he would otherwise have done, for he is a most spiteful creature, but the magic nuts saved you from him, as well as from a number of dangers of this island of which you do not know. You are the first living man who has ever entered so far. Now let us sit down, and keep well out of reach of the grasping hands, and I will tell you my story.”

===Chinese===
她静静地听完，说道：“你这条命，是齐尔巴尔坚果救下的。它们施过魔法，那只黄鹰才没能当场要你的命——换了旁的时候，它早下手了，它是个顶顶歹毒的东西。这魔法坚果不但护你躲过它，也护你躲过了这岛上种种你还不知道的凶险。你是头一个走得这么远的活人。来，咱们坐下吧，离那些抓人的手远些，我把我的身世讲给你听。”

===Original===
Rupert sat down, and the tigress arranged herself at his feet, and curled her tail beneath her, and began as follows:—

===Chinese===
鲁珀特坐下了，母虎在他脚边安顿下来，尾巴收拢在身下，开口讲道：——

===Original===
> My name was Trevina. I was the youngest of three sisters, and my father was a miller. I was the most beautiful of the three. We lived in a mill on the bank of a river, close by the sea, and my greatest amusement was to search about on the rocks for the strange animals and seaweed I found there.

===Chinese===
> 我原本叫特雷维娜（Trevina），是三姐妹里最小的，父亲是个磨坊主。我们三个数我最美。我们住在河边一座磨坊里，离海很近；我最大的乐趣，就是在礁石间寻寻摸摸，找那些奇异的动物和海草。

===Original===
> A little way in the sea was a small island, on which great seabirds collected, but where we never went, as there was nothing to be got there, and it was difficult to climb on it, for it was surrounded by sharp rocks. Nevertheless I often looked at it, and wondered what sort of a place it was, and thought I would try and get to it when I could.

===Chinese===
> 离岸不远的海里有座小岛，大群的海鸟聚在那儿，我们却从来不去，因为那上面没什么可捡的，四周又尽是尖利的礁石，难以上去。可我常常望着它出神，猜想那究竟是个怎样的地方，想着一有机会，定要设法上去看看。

===Original===
> One evening as I was walking along the shore, I noticed that I was followed by a large tortoise, who kept close to me, and looked up into my face beseechingly. I did not think it odd, for there were plenty of tortoises among the rocks, and when they were hungry they often followed us, hoping for food. But when I got home I found the same tortoise in my room, and not liking to send away a creature who kept so close to me, I gave it some bread and milk, and a little bit of matting to sleep on. My sisters laughed at me for my new pet, but nevertheless I took great care of my tortoise, and let it follow me where it liked. After a time, however, its affection for me became quite tiresome. I could not move without its following me closely, and even if I fell asleep and woke I found it sitting watching me. I began to feel provoked with it, and declared that if it continued to be so worrying I would take it and throw it into the sea. Would that I had, alas!

===Chinese===
> 一天傍晚，我沿着岸边散步，发觉身后跟着一只大乌龟。它寸步不离地挨着我，仰起脸恳求似的望我。我并不觉得奇怪，礁石间乌龟多的是，它们饿了常跟人走，指望讨口吃的。可我回到家里，竟见那只乌龟待在我房间里。它既这样寸步不离地缠着我，我也不忍把它撵走，便给了它些面包和牛奶，又给了它一小块草席睡觉。姐妹们都笑话我养了这个新宠儿，我却仍旧悉心照料我的乌龟，任它爱跟到哪儿就跟到哪儿。谁知过了一阵子，它那股眷恋劲儿实在叫人厌烦了。我走到哪儿它跟到哪儿，寸步不离；哪怕我睡一觉醒来，也总见它蹲在旁边守着我瞧。我渐渐动了气，声言它再这样纠缠，我就把它捉起来扔进海里。唉，可惜我当初真该那样做！

===Original===
> My father owned a boat on the river, and often on hot afternoons I would step into it, and lie there, idly gazing at the sky, and enjoying the rippling motion, and as it was always safely moored to the side I felt no fear of drifting away.

===Chinese===
> 父亲在河上有一条船。炎热的午后，我常踱进船里，躺在那儿闲望着天，受用那微波轻荡的滋味。船总是牢牢拴在岸边，我一点也不怕它会漂走。

===Original===
> On one unlucky afternoon I went out about sunset, and placed myself comfortably in the cushions at the bottom of the boat, meaning to spend an hour in reading. But I was overcome with the heat, and ere I had been there long fell fast asleep. I slept soundly, and when I awoke I found that it was getting quite dark. Starting up, I saw with terror that the boat was no longer in the river, but had been evidently loosed from its fastenings and had drifted to sea, and was already halfway to the island. I did not know that the tortoise had followed me, but looking on the seat I saw it sitting there, and I felt a positive hatred for it when I saw the self-satisfied leer with which it was regarding me. To my fright and astonishment, it drew near to me, and with a deep bow said—

===Chinese===
> 一个倒霉的下午，日落时分我又上了船，舒舒服服地靠在船底软垫上，打算读一个钟头的书。怎奈暑气熏人，没待多久便沉沉睡去。我睡得正香，一觉醒来，天已擦黑。我惊跳起来，骇然看见船已不在河里——拴绳分明被人解开，船漂进了大海，已经行至半途，快到那座小岛了。我原不知乌龟也跟了来，可朝座板上一看，它正蹲在那儿，那副沾沾自喜、斜眼觑我的模样，叫我打心底里憎恶。叫我惊惶错愕的是，它竟挨近我，深深一躬，说道——

===Original===
> “Be not astonished, lovely Trevina, and be assured that no harm shall come to you. Know that I am no common tortoise, but am the king of the tortoises, and only for your sake have assumed the shape of a common animal, in order that I might dwell near you. I have loved you from the first moment I saw you, and now intend to make you my wife. When we arrive at the island, where my armies are waiting for me, I shall appear in my proper form. Resistance on your part will be useless. From here no one can hear your cries, and as soon as we reach the island my people will bore a hole in the bottom of the boat and sink it, so your return will be impossible. After a day or two I shall transport you to my own home. I should do so at once, were it not that I fear the jealousy of my mother, who wishes me to marry the snake-princess.”

===Chinese===
> “不必惊慌，美丽的特雷维娜，我向你担保，不会有一丝一毫的伤害落在你身上。你要知道，我并非寻常乌龟，乃是乌龟们的国王；只为你的缘故，才化作一副凡兽的模样，好住在你近旁。自见你第一眼，我便爱上了你，如今定要娶你为妻。等到了岛上——我的大军正在那儿候我——我便现出真身。你抗拒也是枉然。这里喊破喉咙也无人听见；一到岛上，我的臣民就会在船底凿个洞把船沉了，你便再也回不去了。过一两日，我便带你回我自己的家。若不是怕我母亲妒忌，我此刻就要带你走——她一心要我娶蛇公主呢。”

===Original===
> I scarcely could hear him to the end with patience. Starting up, I tried to seize him in my hands, meaning to throw him into the water, and so rid myself of his impertinence; but the moment I touched him his whole shape began to change, and swell, and he was quickly transformed into the most hideous figure I had ever beheld. It was like a little black man not more than two feet high, and it carried on its back an enormous shell, while its arms’ and legs were exactly like those of an immense tortoise. And its face! I shudder even now when I remember how hideous it was. The great glaring eyes, the huge mouth, the hideous shrivelled skin! I screamed aloud with all my might, hoping my father or sisters might hear, and, turning my face, covered my eyes with my hands, that they might not be annoyed by the sight of the disgusting creature. But all my shrieks were vain, my voice was blown away by the wind, and no answer came from the shore. On seeing my distress my tormentor only laughed.

===Chinese===
> 我耐着性子，才勉强听他说完。我跳起来，伸手要捉住他，把他扔进水里，好摆脱他的纠缠；谁知手刚一碰，他的身形便开始变化、膨胀，转眼变成我平生仅见的顶丑陋的一个东西。那像是个小黑人，身高不足两英尺，背上驮着一面硕大的壳，臂膀和腿活像一只巨型乌龟的四肢。还有它那张脸！如今想来，我仍要不寒而栗——那双暴突的大眼，那张阔嘴，那皱缩的恶心皮肉！我拼命高声尖叫，指望父亲或姐妹们能听见，又扭过脸去，双手掩住眼睛，免得叫那丑东西污了眼。可我的喊叫全然无用，声音都被风刮散了，岸上没有半点回音。我的折磨者见我这般光景，只是笑。

===Original===
> Gradually the boat sailed to the island, and on looking up I saw that it was covered with tortoises of all sizes, evidently awaiting our arrival. I think I must have fainted. Anyhow, I remember nothing more till I found myself lying on my back in the middle of the island, surrounded by tortoises, and their hideous king leaning tenderly over me. I started up and, bursting through their lines, ran down to the water’s edge, but finding there no means of escape, I flung myself on the ground, crying and sobbing. I felt sure that in time my father and sisters would miss me, and come to the island to search for me, but the notion of being left alone with my horrid tortoise lover till they could come was dreadful. All that night I sat upon a rock close by the water’s edge, weeping bitterly, but still feeling quite sure that in the morning I should see my father coming for me; but morning came and he did not appear, and the day passed away, and still he did not come, and when evening came round again, I began to fear I must give myself up, and had determined that, rather than listen to the odious tortoise-king, I would throw myself into the water, and so put an end to my life. I was standing upon a rock looking into the clear green water, and thinking whether I would wait another day or spring into it at once, and so end my troubles, when I heard a soft voice above me saying, “Trevina,” and looking up, I saw a beautiful seagull floating in the air.

===Chinese===
> 船渐渐漂到岛边，我抬头一看，岛上密密麻麻趴满了大大小小的乌龟，分明是在等我们到来。我想我准是晕了过去。总之，再记起时，我已仰面躺在岛子中央，四周围着乌龟，它们那丑陋的国王正温存地俯身看着我。我跳起来，冲破它们的重围，直奔水边；可那里无路可逃，我扑倒在地上放声大哭。我笃定父亲和姐妹们迟早会发觉我不见了，会到岛上来寻我；可要同我那可怕的乌龟情人在一处熬到他们来，这滋味实在叫人胆寒。那一整夜，我坐在紧靠水边的一块礁石上，哭得好不伤心，却仍笃信天一亮准能望见父亲来接我。可是天亮了，他没有来；一天过尽，他还没有来；待到暮色又合拢来，我开始害怕只得认命了，便横下心：与其听那可憎的乌龟国王摆布，不如投进水里，一了百了。我站在一块礁石上，望着清澈的绿水，盘算着是再等一天，还是即刻跳下去、了结这场苦难。正这时，忽听头顶一个温柔的声音唤“特雷维娜”。我抬起头，见一只美丽的海鸥浮在空中。

===Original===
> “Poor Trevina,” it said, “I can’t carry you away, but I can take a message to your father. I hate the tortoise-king and his mother as much as you do, and would gladly do anything to annoy them. Tell me what I shall say to your father, and I will fly to him tonight.”

===Chinese===
> “可怜的特雷维娜，”它说，“我驮不走你，却能替你捎信给你父亲。你恨乌龟国王和他母亲，我同你一样恨，只要能气气他们，什么都乐意干。告诉我该对你父亲说什么，今夜我就飞去。”

===Original===
> “Will you really, dear, dear gull?” cried I, joyfully. “I shall be grateful to you all my life. Go, then, and tell him that I was stolen by the tortoise-king, and am upon the little island. Tell him to come for me at once, and to bring plenty of guns and swords with which to kill the tortoises.” But scarcely were the words out of my mouth when there was a rumbling noise beside me, and with a bang like thunder a gulf opened in the ground, and there started up through it a hideous figure, very much like the tortoise-king, but bigger and fatter, whom I at once guessed to be his mother. The seagull, at sight of her, gave a shrill cry and flew away.

===Chinese===
> “当真么，亲爱的、亲爱的海鸥？”我喜得喊出来。“我一辈子感激你的大恩。那就去吧，告诉他，我叫乌龟国王掳来了，就在这座小岛上。叫他立刻来救我，多带枪炮刀剑，好杀那些乌龟。”话音未落，我身旁忽地一声闷响，轰隆如雷，地面裂开一道深渊，从中钻出一个丑东西，同乌龟国王一模一样，只是更高更肥——我立刻猜到，这便是他的母亲。海鸥一见她，尖叫一声飞走了。

===Original===
> “So, girl,” she cried, in a dreadful voice, “not content with rejecting my son’s noble offers, you would try to put an end to his life. It is lucky, indeed, for him that, with a mother’s care, I have been watching him and you, when he thought me far away. I was sure no good would come of it, when he honoured you, a common human being, with his love, instead of offering it, as I wished, to the snake-princess. But now you shall be punished. Bitterly may you regret your unfeeling conduct.”

===Chinese===
> “好啊，丫头，”她声音可怕地喊道，“拒绝了我儿高贵的求婚还不算，还想谋害他的性命。亏得我做母亲的处处留心，一直盯着他和你——他还当我远在天边呢。他放着蛇公主不聘——我原是这么指望的——偏把爱情施舍给你这么个凡人，我早料着没有好下场。如今该你受罚了。你这无情无义的行径，且叫你后悔个够！”

===Original===
> At this moment I saw the tortoise-king coming in haste towards us, waddling as fast as his little short legs and heavy shell would let him.

===Chinese===
> 正这时，我见乌龟国王急急赶了来，迈着那双小短腿，驮着沉重的壳，一摇一摆，能多快就多快。

===Original===
> He turned to his mother, and, falling on his knees before her, tried to calm her rage—but in vain. She continued, more fiercely than before—

===Chinese===
> 他转向母亲，跪倒在她面前，想平息她的怒火——却是枉然。她反倒愈发凶狠地说下去——

===Original===
> “Yes! you shall be well punished, for you shall become a tigress; and, left by yourself in the enchanted land, you will wish you had been grateful to my son for his kindness in offering to make you his wife.” Then she waved her black hands in the air, and I felt a dreadful change coming upon me. Hair was growing all over me. My arms became forelegs like those of a tiger. I felt that a tail was beginning to sprout out behind.

===Chinese===
> “对！你要好好受罚：你要变成一头母虎，独自撇在这魔法之地，到那时你就懊悔了，懊悔不曾感激我儿的恩典——他好心好意要娶你为妻呢。”说罢她朝空中挥动黑色的双手，我登时觉得一身可怖的变化袭来：遍身生出毛来，双臂变成虎的前腿，只觉身后一条尾巴正破体而出。

===Original===
> “And now,” cried the tortoise-queen, her eyes sparkling with spiteful pleasure, “you may be very thankful if you remain in your present condition; but if you only eat one mouthful of flesh, you will become a real tigress, and then you can never regain your proper form. As it is, the only way by which you can ever recover your own shape is by being beaten till you bleed, by a man with the rods that grow beneath the Hair Tree.”

===Chinese===
> “还有呢，”乌龟王后喊道，眼里闪着恶毒的快意，“你能保住眼下的模样，就算万幸了；可你只要吃一口肉，就会变成真母虎，再也休想恢复原身。照眼下这样，你若想恢复人形，唯一的法子，就是叫一个男人用发之树底下长出的棒子，把你打得见了血才成。”

===Original===
> I could not speak: I tried, but my voice was choked with tears. I clasped my hands, but found that they would not close on account of the claws which were growing at the ends. I threw myself on the ground at the Queen’s feet to beg for mercy, but I was not allowed to remain long, for, taking me by the nape of my neck, she carried me quickly through the air, and did not stop till we reached this island, where she flung me on the ground with a spiteful laugh, and vanished. For some time I lay in silent misery, till roused by the voice of the seagull calling me by name, and raising myself, I saw it hovering near me.

===Chinese===
> 我说不出话：我想说，喉咙却叫泪水哽住了。我想合起双手，指尖却生出利爪，怎么也合不拢。我扑倒在王后脚边求她开恩，却不容我多待——她一把攥住我的后颈，带我腾空疾飞，一口气飞到这座岛上才停下，把我往地上一掼，狞笑一声，倏忽不见了。我默默躺着，凄凄惶惶，不知过了多久，直到听见海鸥唤我的名字才回过神来。我撑起身子，见它正盘旋在我近旁。

===Original===
> “Poor Trevina,” he said mournfully, “you are worse off now than you were before; but keep a good heart. You will some time recover your proper form, I feel sure; but be sure you eat nothing but herbs.”

===Chinese===
> “可怜的特雷维娜，”它悲切地说，“你如今比先前更苦了；可要打起精神来。我深信你终有恢复原身的一天；只是千万记住，除了青草野菜，什么也不要入口。”

===Original===
> I thanked the seagull, and tried to feel more cheerful; but my case seemed a hard one, for how was ever any man to come to this enchanted ground? And if anyone did get so far, I knew there must be great difficulty in getting the rods from the Hair Tree.

===Chinese===
> 我谢过海鸥，强打精神；可我的处境实在艰难——这魔法之地，凡人哪里到得了呢？纵然真有人到得这里，我知道，要从发之树上取那些棒子，也必是难上加难。

===Original===
> You are the first man I have seen since I was brought here; and since that time I have lived on grass and herbs. Every day have I wept afresh at the thought that I should never again see my dear home or my father and sisters; and my joy, therefore, on seeing you knew no bounds.”

===Chinese===
> 自从被掳到这里，你是我见到的头一个人；这些年我全靠青草野菜活命。每天我一想起再也见不着亲爱的家、见不着父亲和姐妹，就要重新哭一场。所以，见了你，我的欢喜真是没有了边。

===Original===
As she finished, the tigress turned on one side to wipe away a tear, and Rupert was scarcely less moved than she by the story of her misfortunes.

===Chinese===
故事讲完，母虎侧过身去拭泪；她的遭遇，令鲁珀特也几乎同她一样伤心。

===Original===
“But how can I help you, poor Trevina?” said he, after a pause.

===Chinese===
“可我怎么帮得了你呢，可怜的特雷维娜？”他顿了顿，说。

===Original===
“Help me!” cried the tigress, her eyes sparkling with joy. “Are you not searching for the Hair Tree, and do not the rods with which I am to be beaten grow around it? Dear, kind sailor, you will surely not refuse to pick one and beat me with it.”

===Chinese===
“帮我！”母虎喊起来，眼里闪着欢喜的光。“你找的不正是发之树么？要用来打我的那些棒子，不就长在它周围么？亲爱的好心肠的水手，你总不会不肯替我折上一根，用它打我一顿罢。”

===Original===
Rupert, of course, could not refuse to do as she asked, but the thought of having to beat her filled him with disgust. After a pause she arose, and bade him do the same.

===Chinese===
鲁珀特自然不忍回绝她的请求，可一想到要动手打她，心里就满不是滋味。歇了片刻，她站起身来，也叫他起身。

===Original===
“Now,” she said, “it is time for you to continue your journey. I cannot go with you farther, but you must continue straight ahead, turning neither to the right nor left, and take the greatest care you can of the zirbal nuts, for they are the best protection you can have against every sort of spell. You will find me here on your return, but be sure you do not forget to bring with you some of the rods which grow underneath the Hair Tree.”

===Chinese===
“好了，”她说，“你该上路了。我再不能陪你往前去。你只管照直走，不偏左，不偏右，千万看管好那些齐尔巴尔坚果——天下的法术，数它最能护你。你回来时我就在这儿等你，可千万记住，别忘了带上发之树底下长出的几根棒子。”

===Original===
Rupert promised, and said adieu, and went on his way; but he was so full of thoughts of poor Trevina and her sorrows that he had almost forgotten the Queen’s hair and the reward, and was more anxious to find the wonderful Hair Tree, that he might beat the tigress with the rods he found there, than to gather the hair seeds for the Queen.

===Chinese===
鲁珀特应承下来，道了别，继续赶路；他满心都是可怜的特雷维娜和她的苦难，几乎把王后的头发和赏金都忘了。此刻他一心想寻到那奇妙的发之树，好用树下的棒子解救母虎，倒把替王后采头发种子放在了后头。

===Original===
As he went on he found the path growing narrower and narrower, and the rocks on each side grew larger and larger, and began to arch over the top of the path, and to darken it. All round the wind moaned piteously, and from far off he could hear a sound like running water. At last he came to a place where the rocks blocked up the path, and in them he perceived a small door. He pushed it open, and stepped through into a long dark narrow passage, down which he-walked. Presently a faint light became visible, and after a time he found himself in front of a high wall, so high that he could not see to the top, in which was a gate on which was written, in letters of gold—

===Chinese===
他一路走去，只觉小径越走越窄，两侧的岩壁越涨越大，渐渐在头顶合拢成拱，把路遮得幽暗下来。四野的风呜呜咽咽，好不凄惶；远远地，他听得见流水一般的声音。终于走到一处，岩石堵死了去路，岩壁上现出一扇小门。他推门而入，跨进一条又长又黑又窄的甬道，一路走去。不多时，前方透出微光；又走了一阵，他来到一堵高墙跟前——墙高得望不见顶——墙上有一扇门，门上几个金字——

===Original===
> “Only he who knows can enter here,
 

 Yet only he can know, who enters without fear.”

===Chinese===
> “唯有知晓之人，方能进入此门；
 

 唯有无惧而入之人，方能知晓。”

===Original===
Then Rupert remembered what the bird had said, and stood in front of the gate and said—

===Chinese===
鲁珀特记起鸟儿的话，便站到门前，念道——

===Original===
> “I know, I know;
 

 Inside the wind doth blow,
 

 Inside the waters flow;
 

 This makes the Hair Tree grow—
 

 I know, I know.”

===Chinese===
> “我知道，我知道；
 

 里面有风吹不休，
 

 里面有水长长流，
 

 风吹水淌，发之树才长得秀——
 

 我知道，我知道。”

===Original===
In a moment the door flew open, and Rupert stepped inside and looked about him.

===Chinese===
门顿时豁然大开，鲁珀特跨进门去，环顾四周。

===Original===
He found himself standing at the beginning of a long flat tract of country, but for a moment or two he was so dazzled by the light which enveloped everything, that he could scarcely see.

===Chinese===
他发觉自己站在一片平展展、望不到头的旷野的入口，只是那笼罩万物的亮光晃得他晕眩，一时间几乎什么也看不清。

===Original===
This light was quite unlike anything he had ever seen; it was of a pale clear green colour, and as his eyes grew a little more used to it, he perceived that it filled every nook and corner, and the only shade anywhere was thrown by the black rays of a black sun in the sky!

===Chinese===
这光他平生从未见过：淡而清的绿色。等眼睛渐渐惯了，他看出它充塞了每一个角落；而全天下的荫翳，竟只有天上一个黑太阳射出的黑光投下的影子！

===Original===
It was very strange. He rubbed his eyes and looked again, but there it was sure enough. The sun was coal black, and threw out long black rays, while everywhere else was light.

===Chinese===
真是奇极了。他揉揉眼睛再看——可不是么，那太阳当真是煤一般黑，射出长长的黑光，四下里却是一片通明。

===Original===
A little in front was a black river flowing around an island, in the centre of which was a mound where grew a tree—such a tree! At sight of it Rupert’s heart gave a great bound, and he felt as if he would go mad with joy, for he knew when he saw it he had come to the end of his journey. On that tree grew no leaves, but it was covered with soft fine hair, which grew from all its branches and fell in waves to the ground. He could not see it very plainly, for the river was wide; but he saw that the island was covered with plants of various kinds. He sat down on the pebbly bank of the river, gazing across and wondering if the water were deep, or how he should manage to cross to the other side. While he was sitting thinking, he put his hand into his pocket and drew out a zirbal nut. Just as he was looking at it, he heard a rippling of the water, and perceived an immense swan swimming along towards him. It was as large as a horse, and brilliant yellow all over, with eyes of shining red that shot out sparks of fire. Rupert sprang back from the bank, for the swan appeared to be very angry, and called out in an awful voice—

===Chinese===
前面不远处，一条黑河环绕着一座小岛，岛中央隆起一座土丘，丘上长着一棵树——好一棵树！一见这树，鲁珀特的心猛地一跳，欢喜得简直要疯了：他知道，见到这树，自己的旅程便到了头。那树上不长一片叶子，却覆满柔细的头发，从每根枝条上披拂下来，一波一波垂到地上。河面宽阔，他看不十分真切，只见岛上长满了各色草木。他在河岸的卵石上坐下来，隔水凝望，盘算着水深不深，该怎样渡到对岸去。正坐着寻思，他伸手进口袋，摸出一颗齐尔巴尔坚果。正端详间，忽听得水面一阵涟漪，一只硕大无朋的天鹅朝他游来。那天鹅大得像一匹马，通体明黄，一双红眼睛灼灼放光，直迸火星。鲁珀特从岸边一跃后退——那天鹅看去怒气冲冲，声音可怕地喝道——

===Original===
“What man are you? and what do you do here so near the magic Hair Tree?”

===Chinese===
“你是什么人？离魔法发之树这么近，来做什么？”

===Original===
“My name is Rupert,” said the sailor, trembling in every limb, “and I mean no harm; I only wish to gather some of the seeds to bring back the Queen’s hair. I should be much obliged to you if you will tell me how to get over the water, and if it’s deep.”

===Chinese===
“我叫鲁珀特，”水手说，四肢都在发抖，“我没有恶意；只想采些种子，好叫王后的头发长回来。您若肯告诉我这水怎么过去、深不深，我就感激不尽了。”

===Original===
The swan did not answer, but its eyes were fixed on the nut in Rupert’s hand, which it was eyeing greedily.

===Chinese===
天鹅没有答话，一双眼睛却死死盯住鲁珀特手里的坚果，贪婪地瞅着。

===Original===
“What have you there?” it said, after a pause, in a much quieter voice. “Surely that is a zirbal nut. Dear me, it is two thousand years since I tasted one. Give me a bit of that one.”

===Chinese===
“你手里拿的是什么？”过了片刻，它语气平和多了，“那准是颗齐尔巴尔坚果吧。哎呀，我有两千年没尝过这滋味了。分我一点儿尝尝。”

===Original===
“I am sure you are very welcome to it,” Rupert was beginning, but he stopped himself in time, and said instead—

===Chinese===
“您要吃尽管……”鲁珀特话到嘴边，及时收住，改口道——

===Original===
“If you will take me on your back across the river, and wait for me and bring me back after I have got some hair seeds, I will give you this one, but on no other condition.”

===Chinese===
“您若肯驮我过河，在那边等我采完头发种子，再驮我回来，这一颗就归您——别的条件一概免谈。”

===Original===
The swan stopped, and thought, and then said, “Very well;” and swimming close to the bank, beckoned to Rupert to get on his back. He obeyed, and the swan swam swiftly over the water. As it approached the farther side, it turned its long head and said—

===Chinese===
天鹅顿了顿，想了想，说：“好吧。”它游近岸边，示意鲁珀特上背。他依言上了去，天鹅破水疾游。将近对岸时，它转过长颈，说——

===Original===
“Whatever you do, don’t let the lip-flowers kiss you. They are sure to ask to. But they won’t really want to kiss. They will only bite a piece out of your cheek; they are terribly greedy.”

===Chinese===
“千万记住，别让那些嘴唇花（lip-flowers）亲你。它们必定要开口求你。可它们哪里是真想亲，它们只会从你腮帮子上咬下一块肉来；它们贪心透了。”

===Original===
The sailor thanked the swan for its advice, and sprang upon the mound. It was covered with plants bearing such flowers as man had never seen before. Some of them were like mouths—soft red lips folded over the whitest rows of shining teeth; and when Rupert struck one of them by chance, they gave a loud angry cry, whilst all the others burst into such weird laughter that he thought it dreadful to hear. But one and all, as he passed by them, swayed their long stalks towards him, and said, “Let me give you a kiss—only one: let me kiss your cheek.” But he minded what the swan had said, and, keeping carefully out of their reach, turned to examine the other flowers. Some were like delicate waxen ears, and these, with their dark green leaves, he did not think at all ugly. But the prettiest of all grew on long slender stalks, and bent like lilies, and their flowers were like human eyes. Big eyes, small eyes, blue eyes, brown eyes, black eyes, hazel eyes, eyes with long black lashes, eyes with scarcely any, eyes with heavy lids—all sorts of eyes, all looking curiously at him. He thought them so pretty that he determined to gather some, and on coming to a very beautiful bright blue eye, he put his hand to the stalk to break it off, but thereupon it wept such floods of tears that the sailor felt sorry for it, and left it glistening on its stem, whereat all the lips burst into a chorus of shrill laughter. But now he was nearing the wonderful Hair Tree, and could think of nothing else. All round it grew a double row of dark green plants, with long stiff leaves, from whose centres grew tall solid silver rods in the place where flowers should be. These rods stood so near together that they made quite a compact railing around the Hair Tree, and it was impossible to get to it without passing through their line.

===Chinese===
水手谢过天鹅的指点，纵身跃上土丘。丘上长满草木，开的花是人间从未见过的。有的花像一张张嘴——柔软的红唇掩着雪白锃亮的牙齿；鲁珀特不小心碰了一朵，它便怒声大叫，其余的花也一齐爆出怪笑，笑声凄厉，听得人心头发毛。可他从旁边经过时，那些花一齐摇着长长的花茎朝他俯过来，说：“让我亲亲你——就亲一下：让我亲亲你的脸蛋。”他牢记天鹅的话，小心躲开它们够得着的地方，转身去看别的花。有的花像一只只精致的蜡做的耳朵，配上墨绿的叶子，他倒不觉得丑。可最耐看的，是长在细长花茎上的那一种，像百合似的弯着颈子，花朵却是一只只人的眼睛。大眼睛，小眼睛，蓝眼睛，褐眼睛，黑眼睛，榛色的眼睛，长着长长黑睫毛的眼睛，几乎没有睫毛的眼睛，眼皮厚厚的眼睛——形形色色的眼睛，全都好奇地望着他。他觉得这些实在太美，决意采上几朵；走到一只极美的亮蓝色眼睛跟前，他伸手去折花茎，那眼睛却顿时泪如泉涌，水手心一软，只好让它亮晶晶地留在茎上；这一来，所有的嘴唇一齐爆出尖利的大笑。可这时他已走近那奇妙的发之树，心里再也容不下别的了。树四周生着两圈深绿色的草木，叶子又长又硬，叶丛正中本该开花的地方，却抽出一根根高挺结实的银棒。银棒根根相接，密密匝匝，恰似在发之树四周围了一道栏杆，不越过这道防线，谁也近不得树身。

===Original===
Drawing close to them, the sailor examined them carefully, to see where they could be gathered, for doubtless these were the rods of which Trevina spoke. They seemed to be fastened into the plants with a sort of hinge, and beneath this Rupert took hold of one to break it off, but no sooner had he begun to pull, than the rod, swinging on its joint, dealt him a smart blow on the face, which made him stagger back, and all the lip flowers laughed again.

===Chinese===
水手凑近前去，细细端详，想看出从哪里下手采——这必定就是特雷维娜说的那些棒子了。银棒像是用一种铰链连在草木上；鲁珀特从铰链下面抓住一根，想把它折下来，谁知刚一使劲，那棒子便顺着关节一甩，狠狠抽在他脸上，抽得他踉跄倒退，嘴唇花又齐声大笑起来。

===Original===
“Oh! zirbal nuts,” he cried, taking one from his pocket, “you have helped me before, help me again.” No sooner had he spoken than all the lips cried out, “A nut, a nut—give us a nut!”

===Chinese===
“啊！齐尔巴尔坚果，”他喊道，从口袋里掏出一颗，“你们从前帮过我，再帮我一回罢。”话音刚落，所有的嘴唇齐声喊起来：“坚果，坚果——给我们一颗坚果！”

===Original===
Then a beautiful rosy mouth, growing on a very tall stalk, turned to the others and called, “Silence!” and bending towards Rupert said, “Listen; take out your penknife and cut that nut in pieces, and give them to us, and we will bite off some of the silver rods for you, so that you can pass through to the Hair Tree.”

===Chinese===
这时，一只生在极高花茎上的美丽红润的嘴转向同伴，喝了一声“静一静”，又俯向鲁珀特说：“听着：掏出你的小刀，把那颗坚果切成小块分给我们，我们就替你咬断几根银棒，好让你进到发之树跟前。”

===Original===
Rupert agreed, and taking out his penknife began to chop up the nut, whilst a number of mouths turned themselves to the silver rods and began to bite their stalks. It was vain for the rods to slash about, they could not hurt the lip-flowers, who went on steadily gnawing till half a dozen rods lay on the ground, leaving a clear path through to the Hair Tree.

===Chinese===
鲁珀特依了，掏出小刀把坚果切碎；一众嘴巴则转向银棒，咬起棒根来。银棒左抡右扫也是徒劳，伤不着嘴唇花分毫；那些嘴不紧不慢一路啃下去，直到六根棒子横在地上，空出一条直通发之树的路来。

===Original===
Rupert at once gave the pieces of nut to the mouths (which opened greedily to receive them), and walked up to the tree and stood beneath it.

===Chinese===
鲁珀特立刻把碎坚果分给那些嘴（它们贪婪地张开接住），然后走到树前，立在树下。

===Original===
What a wonderful tree it was! The hair rippled down from all its branches, and was of all colours—black outside, and growing lighter and lighter till, quite near the trunk, it was of fine pure gold. Rupert took hold of it and passed it through his fingers. How soft and thick it felt! What would not the court ladies have given for even a tiny branch of it. Then he thought of the Queen’s hair, and turned to look for the seeds.

===Chinese===
好一棵奇树！头发从根根枝条上波纹似的披泻下来，颜色层层不同——外圈乌黑，越往里越浅，到紧挨树干的地方，竟是纯金的成色。鲁珀特抓起一把，任它从指间流过——好软，好厚实！这样一小枝，那些宫廷命妇有什么不肯拿出来换的！这时他想起王后的头发，便转身去找种子。

===Original===
He found them in little pods growing close to the branches, and at once tried to pull them off. But he found that they grew on hairs so long and thick that he had to take out his knife and cut them, and even then it was a long time before he could gather any quantity. At last he succeeded in picking a handful, and, wrapping them carefully in his handkerchief, placed them in his bosom. When he turned to go, the eye-flowers had all closed, and were evidently fast asleep; the lips, too, were shut and still, and said nothing as he passed them. The black sun was fast sinking, and the black rays had become longer and darker. Rupert walked quickly down to where the yellow swan was waiting, carrying the silver rods with him.

===Chinese===
他找到了：种子结在紧贴枝条的小豆荚里。他动手去摘，却发现豆荚长在又长又粗的头发上，只得掏出刀来割；即便如此，也过了好半天才采到一些。末了他总算满满采了一把，仔仔细细包进手帕，揣进怀里。转身要走时，那些眼睛花（eye-flowers）全都阖上了，分明睡熟了；嘴唇也闭着，一声不响，任他走过。黑太阳正急急沉落，黑光愈拖愈长、愈染愈浓。鲁珀特带着银棒，快步走下土丘，到黄天鹅等候的地方去。

===Original===
“Make haste,” said the swan; “what a long time you’ve been! It’s getting quite light, and I want to go to sleep.”

===Chinese===
“快点吧，”天鹅说，“你去得好久！天快亮了，我要睡了。”

===Original===
“To sleep!” said the sailor, staring. “Why, one goes to sleep when it gets dark, one wakes when it’s light.”

===Chinese===
“睡！”水手瞪着眼说，“怪事，天黑了才睡觉，天亮了才醒来呀。”

===Original===
“Does one?” said the swan, scornfully. “That would be very foolish; where would be the use of going to sleep, and shutting one’s eyes in the dark? The dark never hurts anyone’s eyes. Of course one shuts one’s eyes when it’s too light, and it would hurt one to keep them open.”

===Chinese===
“是么？”天鹅轻蔑地说，“那就太蠢了；天黑了睡觉闭眼，有什么用处？黑暗从来不伤人的眼睛。光太亮了自然要闭眼，硬睁着才伤眼睛呢。”

===Original===
Rupert was silent, not knowing what to say, and the swan swam with him quickly across the water.

===Chinese===
鲁珀特不作声了，不知说什么好；天鹅驮着他，飞快游过水面。

===Original===
“Now,” he said, as he landed him on the other side, “give me my nut, and take my advice, go away as quickly as you can. It’s getting so light that soon you won’t be able to see anything for the glare.”

===Chinese===
“好了，”它把他送上对岸，说，“把坚果给我吧。听我一句劝，能走多快就走多快。天亮得这么急，转眼你就什么都看不见了，晃的。”

===Original===
Rupert obeyed, and turned at once to go back, for a dull glaring light was beginning to spread everywhere as the black sun sank, and no sooner had it disappeared than the light became so intense that he could scarcely see, and had to shade his eyes with his hands as he felt his way to the door in the wall. This time no speech on his part was necessary, for it stood wide open, though it shut with a loud bang as soon as he had passed through it. He walked as quickly as he could down the long dark passage, and out into the broad daylight again, and there he sat down to think over all the strange things he had seen. He would have believed it all a dream, but for the seeds in his bosom and the rods at his side. And there, just as he had left them, were the long rows of sunflowers with the beautiful faces looking over their tops, and the white arms and hands stretching out from their sides; and then, after a few minutes, he saw the tigress coming towards him, walking very slowly, as if she had scarcely strength to move, and looking very thin.

===Chinese===
鲁珀特从谏如流，转身便往回走。黑太阳下沉，一片炫目的光已四处漫开；太阳一没，光便烈得他几乎睁不开眼，只得用手遮着，摸索着走到墙边那扇门前。这一回不必再念什么咒语，门早已大敞四开；只是他一穿过，门便砰的一声关上了。他尽快穿过长长的黑甬道，重新走进朗朗天日里，坐下来，把所见种种怪事细细回味。若不是怀里的种子和身边的银棒，他真要把这一切当作一场梦。那边，长长的向日葵还同他离开时一样，美丽的脸庞从花顶上探出来，雪白的手臂从茎侧伸出来。过了几分钟，他望见母虎朝他走来，走得极慢，仿佛挪一步的力气都没有了，瘦得形销骨立。

===Original===
“At last you are come,” she cried, when she saw him; “how long I have waited for you!”

===Chinese===
“你可算回来了，”她一见他便喊道，“我等了你多久啊！”

===Original===
“Long!” said Rupert, staring with surprise; “why, I have not been gone many hours.”

===Chinese===
“多久！”鲁珀特吃惊地瞪着眼说，“咦，我走了没几个钟头呀。”

===Original===
“Hours!” said the tigress, feebly; “why, you have been gone six months.”

===Chinese===
“几个钟头！”母虎有气无力地说，“咦，你走了六个月了。”

===Original===
“Six months!” cried Rupert, “and it has seemed to me like two hours.”

===Chinese===
“六个月！”鲁珀特喊道，“可我只觉得像两个钟头。”

===Original===
“But you have returned, and have brought the rod with you,” she said, joyfully. “So now all is right. But I am very hungry, for I have not been able to find any grass or herbs for a week. So now make haste and beat me at once.”

===Chinese===
“可你到底回来了，棒子也带回来了，”她欢喜地说，“这下什么都好了。只是我饿坏了，我一个星期没寻着青草野菜了。快，快动手打我罢。”

===Original===
“Beat you, you poor starved creature!” cried the sailor, looking pityingly at her; “beat a poor thing who has scarcely strength to crawl! I wouldn’t do such a thing for the world!”

===Chinese===
“打你，你这可怜的饿坏了的家伙！”水手喊道，怜惜地望着她，“打一个连爬都快爬不动的可怜虫！打死我也干不出这样的事！”

===Original===
“Beat me—beat me, I tell you,” called the tigress, writhing on the ground in front of him. “Beat me at once, or it will be the worse for both of us.” Then she added, in a terrible voice, “Did I not tell you I was very hungry? Beat me at once, or *I shall eat you*!”

===Chinese===
“打我——我说，打我，”母虎喊道，在他跟前的地上翻滚着，“立刻打我，不然咱俩都要遭殃。”随后她声音可怕地加了一句：“我没告诉过你我饿坏了吗？立刻打我，不然*我就要吃了你*！”

===Original===
On hearing this, Rupert made no more ado, but, seizing the silver rod, began to thrash the tigress with all his might. She stood quite still to receive his blows, only every now and then urging him on by calling—

===Chinese===
听了这话，鲁珀特不再迟疑，抓起银棒，使出全身力气朝母虎抽去。她一动不动地站着受打，只是不时喊几声催他——

===Original===
“Harder! Beat harder!”

===Chinese===
“重些！再重些！”

===Original===
Rupert obeyed, and continued to beat till he saw that she was bleeding, and then, just as he was going to fling aside the rod and declare he would beat no longer, her skin began to shrivel, and at last fell to the ground, and there arose from it the loveliest maiden Rupert had ever beheld. Her hair, of burning gold, was worthy of the Hair Tree itself, whilst her shining blue eyes and rosy lips were far more beautiful than the strange flowers that grew about it.

===Chinese===
鲁珀特依言加重了手，一路打下去，直到见她见了血。他正要把棒子一扔，声言再不打下去，她的皮忽然皱缩起来，末了整个儿脱落在地——从那皮里，站起了鲁珀特平生仅见的最可爱的少女。她那头头发，是灼灼的黄金色，配得上发之树本身；那双明亮的蓝眼睛、那红润的嘴唇，更远比树边那些奇花美丽。

===Original===
Rupert stood still staring at her in wonder, but she at once put out her hand and said, “How can I thank you enough for what you have done for me? I am Trevina. Now let us hurry away from this dreadful place as fast as we can, but first give me a nut to protect me from spells.”

===Chinese===
鲁珀特怔怔地站在那里，看得出神。她却立刻伸出手来，说：“你为我做了这一切，叫我怎么谢你才好？我就是特雷维娜。快，咱们赶紧离开这可怕的地方，越快越好；只是先给我一颗坚果，好防身挡法术。”

===Original===
Rupert at once gave her one, and as she fastened it into the bosom of her dress, she turned to him and asked with a smile, “Am I like what you expected?”

===Chinese===
鲁珀特立刻给了她一颗。她把坚果别进衣襟，回头嫣然一笑，问：“我同你想的一样么？”

===Original===
“You are far, far more beautiful,” answered he; “is it really possible that you were that tigress?”

===Chinese===
“你比我想的还要美得多，美得多，”他答道，“你当真就是那头母虎么？”

===Original===
“I was indeed that tigress,” said Trevina, as they began to walk quickly to the shore; “and should most likely have remained so but for you. As it is, I shall not feel comfortable till we are off this dreadful island; although the zirbal nuts are the best protection we could have.”

===Chinese===
“我千真万确就是那头母虎，”特雷维娜说。两人快步朝岸边走去。“若不是你，我多半一辈子都是它了。虽说齐尔巴尔坚果是天底下顶好的护身符，可不离开这可怕的岛子，我这颗心总放不下。”

===Original===
“Now, indeed, am I thankful that the Queen lost her hair,” cried Rupert, “since it has helped me to set you free, lovely Trevina.”

===Chinese===
“这么说，王后掉了头发，我倒真要谢天谢地了，”鲁珀特喊道，“多亏它，才叫我救出了你，美丽的特雷维娜。”

===Original===
They soon found the little boat, and getting into it sailed away as fast as they could.

===Chinese===
两人很快寻见小船，跳上去，扬帆飞驶而去。

===Original===
After a little time Rupert remembered the bird and his promise, and said to Trevina that he supposed they should soon see him coming for his nuts.

===Chinese===
行了一程，鲁珀特记起那只鸟和他的诺言，对特雷维娜说，想来那鸟不久就要来讨坚果了。

===Original===
“You have just eight nuts left, and you have promised the eagle six,” said Trevina. “By all means give him the six you promised him, but let me entreat you to keep the remaining two. One I have here, and be sure you do not let him have the other. Remember that the moment you have parted from it, he will have as much power over you as he had over the poor Queen. He will beg you for it, but he must not have it.”

===Chinese===
“你如今只剩八颗坚果，又应承了那鹰六颗，”特雷维娜说，“答应它的六颗，尽管给它；只是余下的两颗，千万听我一句，留在手里。一颗在我这儿，另一颗，切记不能给它。记住：你一撒手，它加在你身上的神通，就同它加在那可怜王后身上的一样大了。它必来哀求你，可决不能依它。”

===Original===
Rupert promised, and in a very short time they saw the eagle flying towards them. He flew round them in circles, and at last alighted on the prow of the boat.

===Chinese===
鲁珀特应承了。不多时，他们便望见那鹰朝他们飞来。它绕着船转圈，末了落在船头。

===Original===
“Well,” he said, fiercely, looking towards Rupert, “I see you have the seeds. Now where are my nuts?”

===Chinese===
“好啊，”它目光凶狠地望着鲁珀特，说，“我看你种子是到手了。我的坚果呢？”

===Original===
“Here they are,” said he, taking six from his pocket, and giving them to him. He took them in his claws and hid them in his feathers, but still he did not go.

===Chinese===
“在这儿呢，”他说着，从口袋里取出六颗，递了过去。鹰用爪子接住，藏进羽毛里，却仍不走。

===Original===
“You have yet got one more,” he said; “what are you going to do with it? You do not eat zirbal nuts.”

===Chinese===
“你还剩一颗呢，”它说，“打算怎么处置？你又不吃齐尔巴尔坚果。”

===Original===
“I mean to keep it as a curiosity,” answered the sailor.

===Chinese===
“我想留着当个稀罕物。”水手答道。

===Original===
“But what use will that be?” croaked the bird; “will you not give it to me?”

===Chinese===
“留着有什么用？”鸟哑声说，“你就不肯给我么？”

===Original===
“No, I can’t do that,” said Rupert; “you must be content with what you’ve got.”

===Chinese===
“不行，这可办不到，”鲁珀特说，“得了多少是多少，你也该知足了。”

===Original===
“In my nest at home,” said the eagle, “I have seven little eaglets, and you have only given me six nuts to take to them. Will you not give me your other nut to take to my youngest little eaglet?”

===Chinese===
“我家里窝里，”鹰说，“有七只小鹰崽儿，你只给了我六颗坚果带回去。你那颗，就不能给我捎给我最小的鹰崽儿么？”

===Original===
On hearing this the sailor fingered the nut in his pocket and looked at the bird, and was just going to give it to him, when Trevina laid her hand on his arm to stop him, and turning to the eagle said—

===Chinese===
水手一听，摸着口袋里那颗坚果，望着那鸟，正要递过去，特雷维娜把手按在他臂上拦住，转身对鹰说——

===Original===
“It is no use your asking for the nut, for he will not give it to you. I know quite well that you have no little eaglets at home, that it is all false, and that you only say it to get the nut from him.”

===Chinese===
“这颗坚果你讨也白讨，他不会给你的。我心里明明白白：你家里根本没有什么鹰崽儿，全是编的，你这样说，不过想骗他手里的坚果。”

===Original===
Then the eagle turned, and, giving one fearful shriek, rose into the air and flew away, and Rupert and Trevina never saw him more.

===Chinese===
鹰闻言转过身，发一声可怖的长唳，腾空飞去；鲁珀特和特雷维娜从此再没见过它。

---

===Original===
A whole year had passed away since the Queen had dreamt the strange dream, and still her head was bald; and no one could tell how to make the hair grow again. Men had sought far and wide. The King had sent messengers to every part of the world. All sorts of strange plants had been brought and stranger remedies suggested. But it was quite evident that no one had found the real Hair Tree.

===Chinese===
自王后做了那个怪梦，整整一年过去了，她的头依旧光着；谁也说不出怎样叫头发长回来。人们天南地北寻了个遍，国王的信使派到了世界各地。种种奇花异草献了上来，种种更离奇的方子也提了出来。可谁都看得明白：真正的发之树，还没有人找到。

===Original===
The Queen had been ill for months, and now scarcely ever appeared in public. At last, when she had quite made up her mind that she would never recover her hair, she sent for the King, and told him that she was seriously thinking of shutting herself up for the rest of her life, and would only consent again to appear on the condition that he should order that all the women in the land should have their heads shaved and wear caps just like hers. The King, in great consternation, begged her to reconsider her decision, but she was firm, and as he could not bear the thought of parting with her, he at last consented to publish a proclamation ordering that all the women in the country, from the greatest duchess to the poorest beggar, were to have their heads shaved, in order that the Queen might not be annoyed by the sight of their hair.

===Chinese===
王后已缠绵病榻数月，如今几乎不再露面。到后来，她认定头发再也好不了了，便召来国王，说自己认真打算从此闭门不出，了此一生；若要她再肯露面，只有一个条件：下旨叫全国妇女都剃光头发，戴上同她一样的小帽。国王大惊失色，苦苦求她再想一想，她却铁了心。国王想到从此见不着她，又万般舍不得，末了只得下诏：举国妇女，上至最尊贵的公爵夫人，下至最穷苦的女乞丐，一概剃去头发，免得王后见了她们的头发心烦。

===Original===
Great was the anger and discontent with which this order was received, but the people dared not disobey the King, so after a great deal of grumbling they agreed to submit in silence, and a day was settled on which the shaving was to take place, for the Queen wished it to be performed in public.

===Chinese===
这道旨意惹得怨声四起，群情激愤；可百姓不敢违抗国王，嘟囔归嘟囔，末了只得默然听命。剃发的日子定了下来——王后要当众举行这场大典。

===Original===
At her suggestion the King had an immense scaffold put up in the marketplace, and on it the court barber was to stand, whilst all the women, from the highest to the lowest, came before him in turn and had their heads shaved quite close. State seats were erected on one side, on which the King and Queen and court would sit to watch the shaving.

===Chinese===
依王后的主意，国王在集市上搭起一座巨大的高台，宫廷理发师立于台上，全体妇女无论贵贱，依次上台，剃个精光。一侧设了观礼席，国王、王后和满朝廷臣将坐在那里，观看剃发。

===Original===
When all this was settled, the Queen grew as cheerful as possible. She said it was almost as good as having her own hair back again.

===Chinese===
这一切定妥之后，王后竟欢天喜地起来。她说，这几乎同自己长回头发一样痛快。

===Original===
The different messengers whom the King had sent in search of the Hair Tree still kept returning, each one bringing with him some strange new plant, but nothing that could do the Queen any good; and thus the time passed till the shaving day came.

===Chinese===
国王派出寻树的信使仍陆续归来，个个带回些新奇草木，却没有一样于王后有半分用处。日子就这样过去，剃发的日子到了。

===Original===
All the streets were hung with black, and the chair in which the ladies were to sit to be shaved was hung with black also.

===Chinese===
条条街道挂满了黑纱，妇人们剃发要坐的那把椅子，也蒙着黑纱。

===Original===
Early in the morning a great crowd had assembled, and then the King and Queen came down and took seats, and the shaving was to begin.

===Chinese===
一清早，大群的人便聚拢来；随后国王和王后驾临入座，剃发大典就要开始了。

===Original===
The crier took out his list of the ladies’ names, and called out the first, which was that of a duchess who was quite young and very beautiful. She rose slowly from her seat, sobbing bitterly, and walked towards the black chair.

===Chinese===
传令官取出贵妇名册，唱出第一个名字——是一位公爵夫人，十分年轻，十分美丽。她缓缓离座，悲悲切切地哭着，朝那把黑椅走去。

===Original===
She was dressed in a long black serge dress, without any ornament, but her beautiful white neck and arms were bare, and over her shoulders to her waist rippled her bright soft brown hair. She looked so young and so miserable that there was a general groan at the idea that all her lovely locks must fall.

===Chinese===
她穿一条长长的黑呢裙，不缀一点饰物；秀美的脖颈和手臂裸露着，一头明亮的柔顺褐发从肩头披泻到腰际。她看上去那样年轻、那样凄楚，想到她满头秀发就要落尽，人群里发出一片叹息。

===Original===
With a last sob, the duchess took her seat in the dreadful chair, and, closing her eyes, resigned herself to her fate. The barber sharpened his razor, and was just going to begin, whilst all held their breath from excitement, when a voice was heard crying “*Stop*!” And in a minute all saw a sailor rushing towards the scaffold, panting and out of breath, but holding out something in his right hand.

===Chinese===
公爵夫人呜咽了最后一声，在那把可怕的椅子里坐下，闭起眼睛，听天由命。理发师磨着剃刀，正要下手，众人也个个屏住呼吸——忽听得一个声音高喊“*住手*！”转眼间，只见一名水手直奔高台而来，气喘吁吁，右手高举着一样东西。

===Original===
“*Stop!*” he shouted, as loudly as he could, directly he found breath to speak. “I have got it the hair seeds from the *real Hair Tree*!”

===Chinese===
“*住手*！”他一缓过气来，便扯开嗓子拼命喊。“我拿到了——这是*真正的发之树*上的头发种子！”

===Original===
On hearing this the barber flung away his razor, and the Queen screamed outright.

===Chinese===
一听这话，理发师把剃刀远远扔开，王后失声尖叫起来。

===Original===
The courtiers and the King danced a jig for joy, whilst the duchess who was to have been shaved, threw her arms round the sailor’s neck, and kissed him at once.

===Chinese===
廷臣们和国王欢喜得跳起吉格舞来；那位险些剃了头的公爵夫人张开双臂，搂住水手的脖子，就地亲了他一口。

===Original===
“Only try” cried Rupert, eagerly, “before you shave them; they came from the Hair Tree. I picked them myself.”

===Chinese===
“先试试呀，”鲁珀特急切地喊道，“先试再剃不迟；这是发之树上的种子，我亲手采的。”

===Original===
On hearing this, the Queen, unable to contain her impatience any longer, dashed through the crowd, and pushing the duchess on one side. Sprang into the black chair herself.

===Chinese===
一听这话，王后再也按捺不住，分开人群冲了过来，把公爵夫人推在一旁，自己一跃坐上了那把黑椅。

===Original===
“There is no time like the present,” she cried pulling off her muslin cap, and flinging it to the ground, quite heedless of showing her bare shining head to the crowd. “Put some on yourself, dear sailor, and let us see how they do.”

===Chinese===
“赶早不如赶巧，”她喊道，一把扯下细纱小帽，掷在地上，全不顾把自己光亮的秃头露在众人眼前。“亲自动手撒吧，亲爱的水手，咱们瞧瞧它们灵不灵。”

===Original===
A thrill of excitement ran through the crowd, whilst, amidst a dead silence, Rupert opened the packet, and carefully and slowly sprinkled the seeds over the Queen’s bent head. In a moment a soft fine down began to appear all over it, growing thicker and darker every moment, till it was curly hair, which grew longer and longer as they watched it.

===Chinese===
人群里一阵激动的战栗。万籁俱寂中，鲁珀特打开纸包，小心翼翼、慢慢地把种子撒在王后俯下的头上。顷刻间，满头生出柔软的绒发，越看越浓，越看越深，渐渐变成鬈发，众人眼看着它越长越长。

===Original===
“How does it do?” gasped the Queen. “My head feels very queer. Is it growing?” But she had little need to ask the question, for no sooner were the words out of her mouth than the soft curly locks, of her own old colour, fell over her shoulders, and grew till they reached her feet. At sight of them she shrieked with joy, and fainted, whilst the King and courtiers and all the crowd shouted and hurrahed as if they had gone mad.

===Chinese===
“怎么样了？”王后气都喘不匀了。“我头里痒酥酥的，好生奇怪。长出来了么？”其实她何必问——话音未落，那柔软的鬈发——正是她从前的颜色——已披满双肩，一直长到脚面。一见这头发，她欢喜得尖叫一声，晕了过去；国王、廷臣和满场百姓齐声欢呼呐喊，个个如痴如狂。

===Original===
That night the city was illuminated, and there was a great ball at the palace, and next day the King and Queen and all the court went to witness the wedding of Rupert and Trevina, after which the King presented Rupert with the reward.

===Chinese===
当夜，全城张灯结彩，宫里举行了盛大的舞会。第二天，国王、王后偕满朝廷臣亲临鲁珀特和特雷维娜的婚礼观礼；礼成，国王把赏金颁给了鲁珀特。

===Original===
One of his remaining nuts Rupert gave to the King to hang over the palace door, and defend them from all charms of bad fairies; but the other he and Trevina always kept, which is perhaps the reason they lived so happily together all the rest of their lives.

===Chinese===
剩下的坚果，鲁珀特献了一颗给国王，悬在宫门之上，抵御一切邪恶仙女的妖法；另一颗，他同特雷维娜始终珍藏着——两口儿后来一辈子过得那样和美，说不定正是这个缘故。

### The Seeds of Love / 爱的种子

===Original===
Many years ago, in a country far over the sea, was a little village standing by a great river; and over the river was a bridge, with gates which were opened and shut when carriages and horses went through. A little white cottage stood close beside the bridge, and in it lived an old woman and her two granddaughters, whose business it was to open and shut the heavy iron gates. The woman was very old, and her two granddaughters were the children of her two sons, who were both dead; so the young girls were cousins. They were just the same age, but not the least alike. They were named Zaire and Blanchelys. Blanchelys had gold-coloured hair, and eyes like blue cornflowers, and she laughed and sang from morning till night. Zaire’s hair was black as a raven’s wing, and her eyes were like large sloes. She was called the prettiest girl in all the village, but no one loved her as they did the blue-eyed Blanchelys.

===Chinese===
许多年以前，在远隔重洋的一个国度里，有一座傍着大河的小村庄；河上架着一座桥，桥头装有闸门，车马经过时便开闸闭闸。桥旁紧挨着一座小小的白色农舍，里面住着一位老婆婆和她的两个孙女，开闭那两扇沉重的铁门便是她们的差事。老婆婆年事已高，两个孙女是她两个儿子的孩子，两个儿子都已去世，所以这两个姑娘是堂姐妹。她们俩正好同岁，模样性子却半点不像，一个取名扎伊尔（Zaire），一个取名布兰雪莉丝（Blanchelys）。布兰雪莉丝有一头金色的头发，一双眼睛像蓝色的矢车菊，从早到晚又说又笑、歌声不断。扎伊尔的头发黑得像乌鸦的翅膀，眼睛像两颗饱满的黑刺李。村里人都说她是全村最漂亮的姑娘，可是谁也不像喜爱蓝眼睛的布兰雪莉丝那样喜爱她。

===Original===
The old grandmother did nothing but sit by the fire and knit; so one or other of the girls was always out attending to the gates and receiving the tolls of the passersby. Zaire grumbled at the work, but Blanchelys did it cheerfully, and always said a pleasant word to each of the villagers as they came over the bridge.

One winter the old grandmother was feebler than ever, and on Christmas Eve she called the two girls to her bedside and said﻿—

===Chinese===
老祖母什么活儿也不做，只坐在炉边织毛线；于是两个姑娘总有一个守在外头，照管闸门，向过路人收取过路钱。这差事扎伊尔嫌苦，布兰雪莉丝却做得欢欢喜喜，村人过桥时，她总要对每个人说上一句好听的话。

有一年冬天，老祖母的身体比往年更加衰弱了。圣诞节前夜，她把两个姑娘叫到床前，说道——

===Original===
“My dear children, I feel that my end is now fast approaching, but before I die I have something to say to you both. I trust you will always be good girls, and then you are sure to be happy. I have little to leave you except my blessing, but there is something more I have for each of you. That is these two little candles; they are magic candles, and when you set them alight there will appear to you a fairy who will grant you the wish of your heart. If it is a good wish it will be a good fairy that appears, but if it is a wicked wish it will be a wicked fairy that comes; so I advise you to beware, for bad fairies help none. You must burn your candles alone on a night when there is neither moon nor star, and you can only have one wish, for when that is granted the candle will burn out; but if you will take my advice you will never light them at all. Many, many years ago they were brought over the sea, from a strange land where animals spoke and men and women flew, by a sailor who gave them to my grandmother, who gave them to my mother, who gave them to me. So I have had them all my life, but no one has ever used them, for we all thought that if people live honestly and do their duty, they are sure to be happy without the help of any fairy folk.”

===Chinese===
“亲爱的孩子们，我觉得自己的大限已经近了，可在死之前，我有话要嘱咐你们俩。我信得过你们会永远做好姑娘，那样你们就一定幸福。除了我的祝福，我没什么留给你们的，不过还有一样东西，要一人一件。就是这两支小蜡烛：它们是魔法蜡烛，你们点起蜡烛，就会有一位仙女出现在你们面前，满足你们心里的愿望。若是好愿望，出现的就是好仙女；若是恶愿望，来的就是恶仙女。所以我劝你们千万当心，恶仙女可是谁也帮不了的。你们必须独自一人、在既没有月亮也没有星星的夜里点蜡烛，而且只能许一个愿，因为愿望一实现，蜡烛也就燃尽了。可你们要是肯听我的话，就永远别去点它们。许多许多年前，一个水手把它们从海外带了回来——那是个奇怪的国土，那里的野兽会说话，男男女女都会飞。水手把它们给了我的祖母，祖母给了我的母亲，母亲又给了我。所以它们跟了我一辈子，可谁也没有用过，因为我们一家人都相信：人只要活得清白、尽好自己的本分，不用仙灵帮扶，也一定能得到幸福。”

===Original===
So saying the good woman drew from under her pillow two tiny candles, and gave one to each of the two girls, who stood by her bedside. They took them in great surprise, and Blanchelys stooped down and kissed her, and as she did so the old woman died.

Blanchelys grieved and wept much, for she had loved her well, but Zaire was so busy thinking of her magic candle that she did not grieve for her grandmother’s death, but sat brooding over what great thing she should wish for when she lit it.

===Chinese===
这位善良的老人说着，从枕头底下摸出两支小小的蜡烛，递给站在床前的两个姑娘，一人一支。她们又惊又奇地接了过来。布兰雪莉丝俯下身去吻她，就在吻她的时候，老人咽了气。

布兰雪莉丝伤心极了，哭了好久，因为她深爱着祖母；扎伊尔却一心惦记着她那支魔法蜡烛，祖母死了也不怎么悲伤，只坐在那儿盘算：点蜡烛的时候，该许一桩什么样的大愿才好。

===Original===
“I will keep it till I know of something I long for very much indeed,” she said to herself. So she put the candle safely away; and Blanchelys put hers away also, meaning to take her grandmother’s advice, and never to light it. So the two girls lived in the same little cottage, going out as before to open the gates for the passersby. On the other side of the river was a grand castle which belonged to the King. Long ago he used to stay there to hunt, but now he was grown too old, and the castle was never used. One day the girls heard that the King’s son was coming, and all the village was to be decorated in his honour. The first day he rode through it on his way to the hunt, Zaire and Blanchelys knew that he would cross the bridge; so they both dressed themselves in their very best to come out and open the gates; but Zaire said to Blanchelys, “You stand back, and let me go first, for, as people say I am the prettiest girl in the village, it is right I should be seen.” So Blanchelys stood behind and looked over her cousin’s shoulder.

===Chinese===
“我要把它收起来，等我知道了自己真正盼望的东西再说。”她自言自语道。于是她把蜡烛妥妥帖帖地收好；布兰雪莉丝也把自己那支收了起来，打定主意听祖母的话，永远不去点它。两个姑娘照旧住在那座小农舍里，照旧出门为过路人开闸关门。河对岸有一座华丽的城堡，是国王的产业。从前国王常去那儿小住行猎，如今他年事已高，城堡便一直空着。一天，姑娘们听说国王的儿子要来了，全村都要张灯结彩地迎候他。国王的儿子头一回穿村去行猎的那天，扎伊尔和布兰雪莉丝知道他必得过桥，两人都穿上自己最好的衣裳，出来开闸；可扎伊尔对布兰雪莉丝说：“你退后些，让我站在前头。人家都说我是全村最漂亮的姑娘，理该让大伙儿看见我。”于是布兰雪莉丝退到后头，从她的肩膀上望出去。

===Original===
She saw the party of riders coming across the bridge, and they were all splendidly dressed in coloured velvets and gold, and in the middle, riding on a snow-white horse, was the King’s son, clad in a suit of burnished gold, that sparkled and shone in the sunlight. His hair, which was darker and redder than his golden dress, hung over his shoulders and stood out around his head like fine wires. On his head was a velvet cap, from which hung a long white feather fastened down by a diamond clasp; and as he smiled and talked to those around him, Blanchelys thought she never had seen anyone so beautiful in her life. In front of the party rode trumpeters, blowing on their trumpets, to clear the way, and behind were servants and pages leading hounds and bearing hawks.

===Chinese===
她看见一队骑手过桥而来，个个穿着彩色的天鹅绒和金缕衣裳，华美极了；队伍当中，国王的儿子骑着一匹雪白的马，周身一袭擦得锃亮的金色衣装，在阳光下熠熠生辉。他的头发比那身金衣颜色更深、更红，披散在肩头，像细细的金丝在头四周支棱着。他头戴一顶天鹅绒帽子，帽上垂着一根长长的白羽毛，用一枚钻石别扣扣住；他微笑着同左右说话，布兰雪莉丝觉得，自己生平从没见过这样俊美的人。队伍前头有号手骑马吹号开道，后面是牵着猎犬、架着猎鹰的仆人和侍童。

===Original===
But none of the party noticed the two girls who stood at the cottage door, and the horses’ feet raised a cloud of dust, which flew into Zaire’s face, and she fell into a passion. “If that is all one gets, forsooth, for opening the gate for the King’s son,” she cried, “I will never do it again.” But Blanchelys stood at the door and watched the party of horsemen till they were quite out of sight, and then she sighed. “I would stand at the gate all day if he would only ride by once,” she said, and her cousin laughed at her scornfully. But when the royal party rode back, Blanchelys opened the gate, and stood and gazed at the King’s son as before, and when she returned into the cottage she wept silently, and when she slept at night she dreamed of the King’s son. Every day he came across the bridge on his snow-white horse as he rode to the hunt, and every day Blanchelys came out and opened the gates and gazed on his face; but he never noticed her, and she sighed as she turned again to the cottage. So the days passed, and Blanchelys grew thin and pale. Zaire laughed at her, and asked what ailed her. “If you lose all your good looks like that,” she said, “you will never get a husband.”

===Chinese===
可这一队人马，谁也没有留意站在农舍门口的两个姑娘。马蹄扬起一片尘土，扑了扎伊尔一脸，她顿时大怒。“好啊，给国王的儿子开闸，原来就落得这么个报答！”她嚷道，“往后再也不干了。”布兰雪莉丝却站在门口，望着那队骑手直到完全看不见了，才叹了口气。“只要他能打这儿经过一回，我就是整天站在闸门口也甘心。”她说。扎伊尔听见了，嗤笑她。等王室一行猎罢回程，布兰雪莉丝又开了闸，照旧站在那儿凝望国王的儿子；回到农舍，她暗暗地哭，夜里睡着了，梦见的还是国王的儿子。他每天骑着雪白的马去行猎，每天打桥上过；布兰雪莉丝每天出来开闸，望着他的脸，可他从来不曾留意她，她只好叹着气转回屋去。就这样一天天过去，布兰雪莉丝瘦了，也白了。扎伊尔笑话她，问她哪儿不舒坦。“你要是照这么损下去，”她说，“可就永远嫁不出去啦。”

===Original===
“I want no husband whom I shall ever wed,” said Blanchelys sadly; and at that Zaire laughed the more.

One night when Zaire was sleeping soundly Blanchelys, who had lain awake all night, rose out of her little bed, and stepping softly to the window, looked out at the night. There were neither moon nor stars, and the night was very dark.

“I must be quick,” said Blanchelys, “for soon the sun will rise.”

===Chinese===
“我想嫁的那一个，我是永远嫁不成的。”布兰雪莉丝悲哀地说；扎伊尔听了，笑得越发厉害了。

一天夜里，扎伊尔睡得正香，布兰雪莉丝却一直醒着。她从小床上爬起来，轻手轻脚走到窗前，望着外面的夜色。那晚无月也无星，夜黑得很。

“我得赶紧，”布兰雪莉丝说，“太阳很快就要升起来了。”

===Original===
So she dressed herself quickly, but she left her hair hanging down her back, and trod noiselessly to the cupboard, and softly opened the door. She took out the candle, and hid it in her bosom.
